\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\mathbb{P}_{[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\Psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\lambda_{2}^{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1}\leq|k|<\lambda_{2}\\
k\in\lambda r\mathbb{Z}^{2}\end{subarray}}\bigl|\widehat{\Psi}(k)\bigr|\lesssim\lambda_{2}^{N}\left(\frac{\lambda_{2}}{\lambda r}\right)^{2}\,,

which matches the desired bound in (
3.11a
). To prove (
3.11a
) in the case that
\lambda_{2}>\lambda
, we simply appeal to the boundedness of
\mathbb{P}_{[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}
on
L^{\infty}
and (
3.5
).

In order to prove (
3.11b
), standard Littlewood-Paley arguments and the above bound for
\mathbb{P}_{[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\Psi
in
L^{\infty}
again give that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{2{\mathsf{d}}}\left\|D^{N}\Delta^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathbb{P}_{[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}\Psi\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\begin{cases}\left(\frac{\min(\lambda_{2},\lambda)}{\lambda r}\right)^{2}\lambda^{\alpha}\lambda_{1}^{2{\mathsf{d}}-(2{\mathsf{d}}-N)}&\mbox{if}\qquad 0\leq N\leq 2{\mathsf{d}}\\
\left(\frac{\min(\lambda_{2},\lambda)}{\lambda r}\right)^{2}\lambda^{\alpha}\lambda_{1}^{2{\mathsf{d}}}\min(\lambda_{2},\lambda)^{N-2{\mathsf{d}}}&\mbox{if}\qquad 2{\mathsf{d}}+1\leq N\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}\,,\end{cases}

where the factor of
\lambda^{\alpha}
is used to absorb endpoint (
p=\infty
) losses, and
\alpha
may be taken arbitrarily close to zero at the cost of changing the implicit constants. Translating the above display to incorporate the notation
\mathcal{M}\left(N,2{\mathsf{d}},\lambda_{1},\min(\lambda_{2},\lambda)\right)
concludes the proof.
∎

We will require
[
5
, Lemma 4.7]
, which lists the geometric properties of deformed intermittent pipe flows.

Lemma 3.7

(
Control on Axes, Support, and Spacing).

Consider a convex neighborhood of space
\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
. Let
v
be an incompressible velocity field, and define the flow
X(x,t)
and inverse
\Phi(x,t)=X^{-1}(x,t)
, which solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Phi+v\cdot\nabla\Phi=0\,,\qquad\Phi_{t=t_{0}}

\displaystyle=x\,.

Define
\Omega(t):=\{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}:\Phi(x,t)\in\Omega\}=X(\Omega,t)
. For an arbitrary
C>0
, let
\tau>0
be a parameter such that

\tau\leq\bigl(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{C+2}\bigr)^{-1}\,.

Furthermore, suppose that the vector field
v
satisfies the Lipschitz bound

\sup_{t\in[t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau]}\left\|\nabla v(\cdot,t)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(t))}\lesssim\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{C}\,.

Let
\mathbb{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r}:\mathbb{T}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a set of straight pipe flows constructed as in Proposition
3.2
and Proposition
3.3
which are
\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}^{3}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r}}
-periodic for
\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq r\leq 1
and are concentrated around axes
\{A_{i}\}_{i\in\mathcal{I}}
oriented in the vector direction
\xi
for
\xi\in\Xi
, passing through the grid-points in item (
2
) of Proposition
3.2
. Then
\mathbb{W}:=\mathbb{W}^{k}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r}(\Phi(x,t)):\Omega(t)\times[t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau]
satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

We have the inequality

(3.16)

\textnormal{diam}(\Omega(t))\leq\left(1+\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\textnormal{diam}(\Omega)\,.

(2)

If
x
and
y
with
x\neq y
belong to a particular axis
A_{i}\subset\Omega
, then

(3.17)

\frac{X(x,t)-X(y,t)}{|X(x,t)-X(y,t)|}=\frac{x-y}{|x-y|}+\delta_{i}(x,y,t)

where
|\delta_{i}(x,y,t)|<\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-1}}
.

(3)

Let
x
and
y
belong to
A_{i}\cap\Omega
, for some
i
, where the axes
A_{i}
are defined above. Denote the length of the axis
A_{i}(t):=X(A_{i}\cap\Omega,t)
in between
X(x,t)
and
X(y,t)
by
L(x,y,t)
. Then

(3.18)

L(x,y,t)\leq\left(1+\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\left|x-y\right|\,.

(4)

The support of
\mathbb{W}
is contained in a
\displaystyle\left(1+\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)2\pi(4n_{\ast}\lambda_{q+1})^{-1}
-neighborhood of the set

(3.19)

\bigcup_{i}A_{i}(t)\,.

(5)

\mathbb{W}
is “approximately periodic” in the sense that for distinct axes
A_{i},A_{j}
with
i\neq j
, we have

(3.20)

\left(1-\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{dist\,}(A_{i}\cap\Omega,A_{j}\cap\Omega)\leq\mathrm{dist\,}\left(A_{i}(t),A_{j}(t)\right)\leq\left(1+\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\mathrm{dist\,}(A_{i}\cap\Omega,A_{j}\cap\Omega)\,.

The following proposition is a variation on the statement and proof of
[
5
, Proposition 4.8]
. For simplicity, we only consider
\xi=e_{3}
. The generalization to other vectors
\xi\in\Xi
follows from incorporating a rotation into the argument; for further details we refer to the final paragraph of the proof of
[
5
, Proposition 4.8]
. The main difference in the new Proposition is that the set on which placements are made now has dimensions
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}\times(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}\times(\lambda_{q+1}r_{1})^{-1}
as opposed to
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{1})^{-1}\times(\lambda_{q+1}r_{1})^{-1}\times(\lambda_{q+1}r_{1})^{-1}
in
[
5
]
.

Proposition 3.8

(
Placing straight pipes which avoid bent pipes).

Let
\lambda_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq 1
be such that
\lambda_{q+1}r_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
be a rectangular prism with the following properties:

(1)

The longest axis of
\Omega
is parallel to
e_{3}
and has length precisely
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{1})^{-1}
.

(2)

There exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}
(bounded independently of
q
) such that the face of
\Omega
which is perpendicular to
e_{3}
is a square of side length precisely
\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}
.

(3)

There exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{P}
such that for any convex subset
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
with
\textnormal{diam}\left(\Omega^{\prime}\right)\leq 2\sqrt{3}\pi\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2}\right)^{-1}
, there exist at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}
segments of deformed
\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}^{3}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r_{2}}}
-periodic pipes of length
4\pi\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2}\right)^{-1}
. Here, by “segments of deformed pipes,” we mean the objects constructed in Propositions
3.2
and
3.3
which satisfy the conclusions (
3.16
)–(
3.20
) from Lemma
3.7
on
\Omega
. Let
\mathsf{P}
denote the union of the supports of the deformed pipe segments.

Then, there exists a geometric constant
C_{*}\geq 1
such that if

(3.21)

\displaystyle C_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}r_{2}^{2}\leq r_{1}\,,

then there exists a set of pipe flows
\mathbb{W}^{k_{0}}_{e_{3},\lambda_{q+1},r_{2}}\colon\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
which are
\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}^{3}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r_{2}}}
-periodic, concentrated to width
2\pi(4\lambda_{q+1}n_{*})^{-1}
around axes with vector direction
e_{3}
, satisfy the properties listed in Proposition
3.3
, and

(3.22)

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\mathbb{W}^{k_{0}}_{e_{3},\lambda_{q+1},r_{2}}\cap\mathsf{P}\cap\Omega=\emptyset\,.

Proof of Proposition
3.8
.

The proof has been streamlined relative to the original version
[
5
, Proposition 4.8]
, although the fundamental ideas remain unchanged. We divide the proof into three steps, in which we count the number of segments of deformed pipe of length
\approx(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}
, then project each segment onto the smallest face of
\Omega
and cover it with squares of size
\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
, and finally use a pigeonhole argument and the bound (
3.21
) to find a shift
k_{0}
satisfying (
3.22
).

Step 1:
To count the number of deformed segments of pipe which may comprise
\mathsf{P}\cap\Omega
, we appeal to assumption (
3
) and volume considerations. The dimensions of
\Omega
imply that
\Omega
is composed of at most
\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2}\cdot r_{2}r_{1}^{-1}
periodic cells of side length
2\pi(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}
. Applying (
3
) with each of these cells implies that the number of distinct segments of pipe of length
4\pi(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}
comprising
\mathsf{P}
is at most

\mathcal{C}_{P}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}\cdot r_{2}r_{1}^{-1}\,.

Step 2:
We now measure the size of the shadows of the deformed segments of pipe when projected onto the face of
\Omega
which is perpendicular to
e_{3}
. First, the length constraint on the segments of deformed pipe implies that the projection of any single segment onto the face of
\Omega
which is perpendicular to
e_{3}
has length at most
4\pi(\lambda_{q+1}r_{2})^{-1}
. Now consider the grid
\mathbb{G}_{\lambda_{q+1},r_{2}}
from Proposition
3.2
, item (
2
). This grid contains squares of diameter
\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
, each of which may contain part of the support of an
e_{3}
-oriented periodic pipe flow, or may be empty, depending on the choice of shift. Applying a covering argument using the above derived length constraint and (
3.19
), we see that there exists a dimensional constant
C_{*}
such that the number of grid squares needed to cover the projection of a single segment is at most
C_{*}r_{2}^{-1}
. Since the number of segments was bounded by
\mathcal{C}_{P}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}\cdot r_{2}r_{1}^{-1}
from Step 1, we see that the
total
number of grid squares needed to cover the projection of
\mathsf{P}
is at most

\mathcal{C}_{P}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}\cdot r_{2}r_{1}^{-1}\cdot C_{*}r_{2}^{-1}\leq\mathcal{C}_{P}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}C_{*}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}r_{1}^{-1}\,.

Step 3:
In order to conclude the proof, we appeal to a pigeonhole argument, made possible by the bound from Step 2. Indeed, we have obtained an upper bound on the number of grid squares which are deemed “occupied” by projections of deformed segments of pipe. Conversely, from Proposition
3.2
, the number of possible choices for the shifts
k_{0}
is
r_{2}^{-2}
. Applying assumption (
3.21
), we conclude by the pigeonhole principle that there exists a “free” shift
k_{0}

such that
none
of the occupied squares intersect the support of
\mathbb{W}_{\lambda_{q+1},r_{2},e_{3}}^{k_{0}}
. Thus we have proven (
3.22
), concluding the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.
Mollification

Let
\phi(\zeta):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a smooth,
C^{\infty}
function compactly supported in the set
\{\zeta:|\zeta|\leq 1\}
which in addition satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(\zeta)\,d\zeta=1,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(\zeta)\zeta^{n}=0\quad\forall n=1,2,...,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}.

Let
\widetilde{\phi}(x):\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be defined by
\widetilde{\phi}(x)=\phi(|x|)
. For
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{R}
, define

\phi_{\lambda}^{(x)}(x)={{\lambda}^{3}}\widetilde{\phi}\left(\lambda x\right),\qquad\phi_{\mu}^{(t)}(t)=\mu\phi(\mu t).

For
q\in\mathbb{N}
, we will define the spatial and temporal convolution operators

(4.1)

\mathcal{P}_{q,x}:=\phi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}}^{(x)}\ast,\qquad\mathcal{P}_{q,t}:=\phi_{\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}}^{(t)}\ast,\qquad\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}:=\mathcal{P}_{q,x}\circ\mathcal{P}_{q,t}.

Lemma 4.1

(
Mollifying the Euler-Reynolds system
)
.

Let
(v_{q},\mathring{R}_{q})
solve the Euler-Reynolds system (
1.2
), and assume that
\psi_{i,q^{\prime}},u_{q^{\prime}}
for
q^{\prime}<q
,
w_{q}
, and
\mathring{R}_{q}
satisfy (
2.8a
)–(
2.21b
). Then, we mollify
(v_{q},\mathring{R}_{q})
at spatial scale
\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}
and temporal scale
\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}
(cf. the notation in (
4.1
)), and accordingly define

(4.2)

\displaystyle v_{\ell_{q}}:=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}v_{q}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}:=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}\mathring{R}_{q}\,.

The mollified velocity
v_{\ell_{q}}
satisfies the Euler-Reynolds system with stress
\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}+\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}
, where the commutator stress
\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}
satisfies the estimate (consistent with (
2.10a
) and (
2.10b
) at level
q+1
)

(4.3)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, and we have that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q-1}^{M}(v_{\ell_{q}}-v_{q})\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\lambda_{q}^{-2}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\tilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
.
Furthermore,
u_{q}=v_{\ell_{q}}-v_{\ell_{q-1}}
satisfies the bound (
2.8
) with
q^{\prime}
replaced by
q

(4.5a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q-1}D^{N}D_{t,q-1}^{M}u_{q}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i}\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\tilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)\,,

(4.5b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q-1}^{M}u_{q}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+1}\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)\,,

for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
. Finally,
\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
satisfies bounds which extend (
2.10
) to the mollified stress

(4.6a)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\psi_{i,q-1}D^{N}D_{t,q-1}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}\delta_{q+1}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\tilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+2}\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\tilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)\,,

(4.6b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q-1}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q-1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\,,\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)

for all
N+M\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

The bounds in (
4.3
)–(
4.5a
), and also (
4.6a
), match those of
[
5
, Lemma 5.1, equations (5.3)–(5.5) and (5.7)]
, and so we omit the proofs. We note that the analogue of estimate (
4.4
) in
[
5
, equation (5.4)]
contains a typo in the sharp material derivative cost. Specifically, one may replace the cost of
\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{i-1}
simply with
\tau_{q-1}^{-1}
(which is actually the estimate that can be proved using the argument in
[
5
]
). The only new estimates which would require a proof are (
4.5b
) and (
4.6b
).

In order to give an idea of how to prove (
4.5b
), we follow the method of proof from
[
5
]
for (
4.5a
). When either
N\geq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
or
M\geq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, an even stronger bound than (
4.5b
) was previously established in
[
5
, Lemma 5.1, equation (5.6)]
. Thus, we only need to consider (
4.5b
) for
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
. We appeal to (
2.3
) and split
u_{q}=\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}w_{q}+\left(\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}v_{\ell_{q-1}}-v_{\ell_{q-1}}\right)
. Since the good term
(\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}-\mathrm{Id})v_{\ell_{q-1}}
was
already
estimated in
L^{\infty}
, cf.
[
5
, equation (5.43)]
with a stronger bound than that required by (
4.5b
), we can consider just the main term
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}w_{q}
. We split
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}w_{q}
as
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}w_{q}=w_{q}+(\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}-\mathrm{Id})w_{q}
. In view of (
2.9b
), which provides a satisfactory bound on
w_{q}
, we are only left with
(\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}-\mathrm{Id})w_{q}
. However, this term was
already
estimated in
L^{\infty}
in
[
5
, equations (5.33)–(5.35)]
, and so no new proof is required. Thus (
4.5b
) is satisfied.

The proof of (
4.6b
) utilizes the same methodology that produced bounds for
\mathcal{P}_{q,x,t}w_{q}
from inductive assumptions on
w_{q}
. Specifically, the material derivative bounds have been relaxed by a factor of
\Gamma_{q}
(the second
\Gamma_{q}
loss coming again from the fact that (
4.6b
) is estimated on the support of
\psi_{i,q-1}
), the spatial derivative bounds have been relaxed from
\lambda_{q}
to
\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
when
N\geq 2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, and the available number of estimates on the un-mollified stress
\mathring{R}_{q}
was much more than
2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, specifically
3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
. We therefore omit any further discussion and refer the reader to the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 5.1]
.
∎

5.
Cutoffs

5.1.
Velocity cutoff functions

For all
q\geq 1
and
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, we construct the following cutoff functions. The specifics of the construction and the proof are contained in
[
5
, Appendix A.2]
. To avoid abuse of notation, here we denote these smooth cutoffs using the capital letters
\Psi_{m,q}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}
, instead of the notation in
[
5
, Appendix A.2]
(which was
\psi_{m,q}
and
\widetilde{\psi}_{m,q}
).

Lemma 5.1
.

For all
q\geq 1
and
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, there exist smooth cutoff functions
\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q},\Psi_{m,q}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,1]
which satisfy the following.

(1)

The function
\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}
satisfies
{\bf 1}_{[0,\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}]}\leq\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}\leq{\bf 1}_{[0,\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}]}
.

(2)

The function
\Psi_{m,q}
satisfies
{\bf 1}_{[1,\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}]}\leq\Psi_{m,q}\leq{\bf 1}_{[\frac{1}{4},\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}]}
.

(3)

For all
y\geq 0
, a partition of unity is formed as

(5.1)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}^{2}(y)+\sum_{{i\geq 1}}\Psi_{m,q}^{2}\bigl(\Gamma_{q}^{-2i(m+1)}y\bigr)=1\,.

(4)

\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}
and
\Psi_{m,q}(\Gamma_{q}^{-2i(m+1)}\cdot)
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}(\cdot)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\Psi_{m,q}\bigl(\Gamma_{q}^{-2i(m+1)}\cdot\bigr)

\displaystyle=\emptyset\quad\textnormal{if}\quad i\geq 2,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\Psi_{m,q}\bigl(\Gamma_{q}^{-2i(m+1)}\cdot\bigr)\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\Psi_{m,q}\bigl(\Gamma_{q}^{-2i^{\prime}(m+1)}\cdot\bigr)

\displaystyle=\emptyset\quad\textnormal{if}\quad|i-i^{\prime}|\geq 2.

(5)

For
0\leq N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, when
0\leq y<\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}
we have

\displaystyle{|D^{N}\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}(y)|}

\displaystyle\lesssim{(\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q}(y))^{1-N/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\Gamma_{q}^{-2N(m+1)}.

For
\frac{1}{4}<y<1
we have

\displaystyle{|D^{N}\Psi_{m,q}(y)|}

\displaystyle\lesssim{(\Psi_{m,q}(y))^{1-N/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\,,

while for
\frac{1}{4}\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}<y<\Gamma_{q}^{2(m+1)}
we have

\displaystyle{|D^{N}\Psi_{m,q}(y)|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{-2N(m+1)}{(\Psi_{m,q}(y))^{1-N/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\,.

In each of the above inequalities, the implicit constants depend on
N
but not
m
or
q
.

Definition 5.2
.

Given
i,j,q\geq 0
, we define

\displaystyle i_{*}=i_{*}(j,q)=i_{*}(j)=\min\{i\geq 0\colon\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\geq\Gamma_{q}^{j}\}.

Note that for
j=0
, we have that
i_{*}(j)=0
.

At stage
q\geq 1
of the iteration (by convention
w_{0}=u_{0}=0
) and for
m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
and
j_{m}\geq 0
, we define

(5.3)

\displaystyle h_{m,j_{m},q}^{2}(x,t):=\sum_{n=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2i_{*}\left(j_{m}\right)}\delta_{q}^{-1}\bigl(\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q}\bigr)^{-2n}\bigl(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{*}(j_{m})+2}\bigr)^{-2m}|D^{n}D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}(x,t)|^{2}\,.

One should view
h_{m,j_{m},q}
as a measurement of the extent to which the amplitude of
D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}
(or its spatial derivatives) exceeds
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{*}(j_{m})}\approx\Gamma_{q}^{j}
, where
\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{j}
is the material derivative cost on the support of
\psi_{j,q-1}
. The extra room of
\Gamma_{q}
in the spatial derivative cost and
\Gamma_{q+1}^{2}
in the material derivative cost accrues extra factors of smallness for high numbers of derivatives. This allows us to eventually plug in a very lossy bound for
|D^{n}D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}|
and still show that the resulting contribution to the sum is very small. To measure the size of
h_{m,j_{m},q}
precisely, we now rescale and plug into a cutoff function.

Definition 5.3

(
Intermediate Cutoff Functions).

Given
q\geq 1
,
m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, and
j_{m}\geq 0
we define
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}
by

(5.4)

\displaystyle\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{m,q+1}\bigl(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2(i_{m}-i_{*}(j_{m}))(m+1)}h_{m,j_{m},q}^{2}(x,t)\bigr)

for
i_{m}>i_{*}(j_{m})
,
while for
i_{m}=i_{*}(j_{m})
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\psi_{m,i_{*}(j_{m}),j_{m},q}(x,t)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{m,q+1}\left(h_{m,j_{m},q}^{2}(x,t)\right).

The intermediate cutoff functions
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}
are equal to zero for
i_{m}<i_{*}(j_{m})
.

The indices
i_{m}
and
j_{m}
were shown in see
[
5
, Lemma 6.14]
and
[
5
, equation (6.27)]
to run up to some maximal values
i_{\rm max}
and
\widetilde{i}_{\textnormal{max}}
, although in the present context, it will be necessary to propagate a much sharper bound on
{i_{\rm max}}
; see Lemma
5.7
. With this notation and in view of (
5.1
) and (
5.2
), it immediately follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{m}\geq 0}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}^{2}=\sum_{i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}^{2}=\sum_{\{i_{m}\colon\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{m}}\geq\Gamma_{q}^{j_{m}}\}}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}^{2}\equiv 1

for any
m
and for
|i_{m}-i^{\prime}_{m}|\geq 2
,

\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}\psi_{m,i_{m}^{\prime},j_{m},q}=0.

Definition 5.4

(
m^{\textnormal{th}}
Velocity Cutoff Function).

For
i_{m}\geq 0
, we inductively define the
m^{\textnormal{th}}
velocity cutoff function

(5.6)

\psi_{m,i_{m},q}^{2}=\sum\limits_{\{j_{m}\colon i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})\}}\psi_{j_{m},q-1}^{2}\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}^{2}.

Informally, one may interpret the definition of the
m^{\textnormal{th}}
velocity cutoff in (
5.6
) as follows. To control
D_{t,q}
, one should split into
D_{t,q-1}
and
u_{q}\cdot\nabla
. The inclusion of
\psi_{j_{m},q-1}
ensures that the cost of
D_{t,q-1}
may be controlled by
\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{j}
and the inclusion of
\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q}
ensures that the cost of
u_{q}\cdot\nabla
may be controlled as well. The index
m
is included for technical reasons, as it is more convenient to control the size of
D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}
for fixed
m
. Therefore, in reality we then control the size of
u_{q}\cdot\nabla
only after incorporating the information provided by the different partitions of unity
\{\psi_{m,i_{m},q}\}_{i_{m}}
for
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
. Whichever value of
i_{m}
is the largest at any point in spacetime then determines the material derivative cost there.

In order to define the full velocity cutoff function, we use the notation

\vec{i}=\{i_{m}\}_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}=\left(i_{0},...,i_{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\right)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1}

to denote a tuple of non-negative integers of length
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1
, and we shall denote

{\mathcal{I}}_{i}=\left\{\vec{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1}\colon\max\limits_{0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}i_{m}=i\right\}.

Definition 5.5

(
Velocity cutoff function).

For
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}(q)
, we inductively define the velocity cutoff function
\psi_{i,q}
as follows. When
q=0
, we let

\displaystyle\psi_{i,0}=\begin{cases}1&\mbox{if }i=0\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then, we inductively on
q
define

(5.7)

\psi_{i,q}^{2}=\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{I}}_{i}}\prod\limits_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\psi_{m,i_{m},q}^{2}.

for all
q\geq 1
.

The sum used to define
\psi_{i,q}
for
q\geq 1
is over all tuples with a maximum entry of
i
. The number of such tuples is
q
-independent since it has been demonstrated in
[
5
, Lemma 6.14]
that
i_{m}\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q)
(which implies
i\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q)
), and
{i_{\rm max}}(q)
is bounded above independently of
q
.

For notational convenience, given an
\vec{i}
as in the sum of (
5.7
), we shall denote

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp\,}\prod\limits_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\psi_{m,i_{m},q}=\bigcap_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{m,i_{m},q})=:\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{\vec{i},q})\,.

In particular, we will frequently use that
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})
if and only if there exists
\vec{i}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+1}
such that
\max_{0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}i_{m}=i
, and
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{\vec{i},q})
.

Proposition 5.6
.

With the definitions of the velocity cutoff functions given in the previous subsection, the inductive assumptions from (
2.11
) and (
2.15
)–(
2.22
) hold.

For the proof, see
[
5
, Section 6]
.
We however must provide a new estimate for
{i_{\rm max}}(q)
in order to prove (
2.12
) and (
2.13
), and we give the details in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.7

(
Maximal
i
index in the definition of the cutoff).

There exists
{i_{\rm max}}={i_{\rm max}}(q)\geq 0
, determined by the formula (
5.12
) below, such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\psi_{i,q}\equiv 0\quad\mbox{for all}\quad i>i_{\rm max}

and

(5.9)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{\rm max}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

for all
q\geq 0
. Moreover, assuming
\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large,
{i_{\rm max}}(q)
is bounded uniformly in
q
as

(5.10)

\displaystyle{i_{\rm max}}(q)\leq 1+{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+\frac{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(b-1)+\beta b}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)b}\,.

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

Assume
i\geq 0
is such that
\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})\neq\emptyset
. We will prove that
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
. From (
5.7
) it follows that for any
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})
, there must exist at least one
\vec{i}=(i_{0},\ldots,i_{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}})
such that
\max\limits_{0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}i_{m}=i
, and with
\psi_{m,i_{m},q}(x,t)\neq 0
for all
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
. Therefore, in light of (
5.6
), for each such
m
there exists a maximal
j_{m}
such that
i_{*}(j_{m})\leq i_{m}
, with
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{j_{m},q-1})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{m,i_{m},j_{m},q})
. In particular, this holds for any of the indices
m
such that
i_{m}=i
. For the remainder of the proof, we fix such an index
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
.

If we have
i=i_{m}=i_{*}(j_{m})=i_{*}(j_{m},q)
, since
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{j_{m},q-1})
, then by the inductive assumption (
2.13
), we have that
j_{m}\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q-1)
. Then using
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-1}<\Gamma_{q}^{j_{m}}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{i_{\rm max}}(q-1)}
and (
2.13
), we deduce that

\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{{i_{\rm max}}(q-1)}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Theta_{q-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q-1}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,.

The last inequality above holds
in light of the parameter inequality

b\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2b}}\leq b{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\beta
, which in turn follows from
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\leq\nicefrac{{\beta}}{{b}}
. Thus, in this case
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
indeed holds.

On the other hand, if
i=i_{m}\geq i_{*}(j_{m})+1
, by the definition of
\Psi_{m,q+1}
in (
5.4
), it follows that
|h_{m,j_{m},q}(x,t)|\geq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})\Gamma_{q+1}^{(m+1)(i_{m}-i_{*}(j_{m}))}
, and by the pigeonhole principle, there exists
0\leq n\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
with

\displaystyle|D^{n}D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{(m+1)(i_{m}-i_{*}(j_{m}))}{\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{*}(j_{m})}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q})^{n}(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{*}(j_{m})+2})^{m}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{m}}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{n}(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{m}+2})^{m},

and we also know that
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{j_{m},q-1})
. By (
4.5b
), the fact that
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, and
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}\leq\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, we know that

\displaystyle|D^{n}D_{t,q-1}^{m}u_{q}(x,t)|

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{n}(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q}^{j_{m}+1})^{m}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{n}(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{*}(j_{m})+1})^{m}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{n}(\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i_{m}})^{m}\,.

The proof is now completed, since the previous two inequalities and
i_{m}=i
imply that

(5.11)

\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\leq 2\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,.

In view of the above inequality, the value of
i_{\rm max}
is chosen as

(5.12)

\displaystyle{i_{\rm max}}(q)=\sup\{i^{\prime}\,:\,\Gamma_{q+1}^{i^{\prime}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\}\,.

With this definition, if
i>{i_{\rm max}}(q)
, then
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}>\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
, and as such
\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})=\emptyset
.
To show that
{i_{\rm max}}(q)
is bounded independently of
q
, note that

\displaystyle\frac{\log(\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})}{\log(\Gamma_{q+1})}={\mathsf{C_{u}}}+\frac{\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(b-1)+\beta b\right)\log(\lambda_{q-1})}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)\log(\lambda_{q})}\to{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+\frac{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(b-1)+\beta b}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)b}\,,

as
q\to\infty
. Thus, assuming
\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large, the bound (
5.10
) holds.
∎

5.2.
Temporal cutoff functions and flow maps

Let
\chi:(-1,1)\rightarrow[0,1]
be a
C^{\infty}
function of compact support which induces a partition of unity according to

(5.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\chi^{2}(\cdot-k)\equiv 1\,.

Consider the translated and rescaled function

\chi\left(t\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}+2}_{q+1}-k\right)\,,

which is supported in the set of times
t
satisfying

(5.14)

\left|t-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}-2}k\right|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}-2}\qquad\iff\qquad t\in\left[(k-1)\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}-2},(k+1)\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}-2}\right]\,.

We then define temporal cut-off functions

(5.15)

\displaystyle\chi_{i,k,q}(t)=\chi_{(i)}(t)=\chi\left(t\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}+2}_{q+1}-k\right)\,.

It is then clear that

(5.16)

\displaystyle{|\partial_{t}^{m}\chi_{i,k,q}|\lesssim(\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}+2}\tau_{q}^{-1})^{m}}

for
m\geq 0
and

(5.17)

\chi_{i,k_{1},q}(t)\chi_{i,k_{2},q}(t)=0

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
unless
|k_{1}-k_{2}|\leq 1
. We define

\chi_{(i,k\pm,q)}(t):=\bigl(\chi_{(i,k-1,q)}^{2}(t)+\chi_{(i,k,q)}^{2}(t)+\chi_{(i,k+1,q)}^{2}(t)\bigr)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},

which are cutoffs with the property that

\chi_{(i,k\pm,q)}\equiv 1\qquad\textnormal{ on }\qquad\mathrm{supp\,}{(\chi_{(i,k,q)})}.

Next, we define the cutoffs
\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
by

\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}(t)=\widetilde{\chi}_{(i)}(t)=\chi\left(t\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}-\Gamma_{q+1}^{-\mathsf{c_{0}}}k\right).

For comparison with (
5.14
), we have that
\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
is supported in the set of times
t
satisfying

\bigl|t-\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}}k\bigr|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+\mathsf{c_{0}}}.

As a consequence of these definitions and a sufficiently large choice of
\lambda_{0}
, if
(i,k)
and
({i^{*}},{k^{*}})
satisfy
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\neq\emptyset
and
{i^{*}}\in\{i-1,i,i+1\}
, then

(5.18)

\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\widetilde{\chi}_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}.

We can now make estimates regarding the flows of the vector field
v_{\ell_{q}}
on the support of a cutoff function. The proofs of Lemma
5.8
and Corollary
5.10
are contained in
[
5
, Section 6.4]
.

Lemma 5.8

(
Lagrangian paths don’t jump many supports).

Let
q\geq 0
and
(x_{0},t_{0})
be given. Assume that the index
i
is such that
\psi_{i,q}^{2}(x_{0},t_{0})\geq\kappa^{2}
, where
\kappa\in\left[\frac{1}{16},1\right]
. Then the forward flow
(X(t),t):=(X(x_{0},t_{0};t),t)
of the velocity field
v_{\ell_{q}}
originating at
(x_{0},t_{0})
has the property that
\psi_{i,q}^{2}(X(t),t)\geq\nicefrac{{\kappa^{2}}}{{2}}
for all
t
be such that
|t-t_{0}|\leq(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+3})^{-1}
, which by (
8.30
) and (
8.20
) is satisfied for
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+5+\mathsf{c_{0}}}
.

Definition 5.9
.

We define
\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t):=\Phi_{(i,k)}(x,t)
to be the flows induced by
v_{\ell_{q}}
with initial datum at time
k{\tau_{q}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i}
given by the identity, i.e.

(\partial_{t}+v_{\ell_{q}}\cdot\nabla)\Phi_{i,k,q}=0\,,\qquad\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,k{\tau_{q}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i})=x\,.

We will use
D\Phi_{(i,k)}
to denote the gradient of
\Phi_{(i,k)}
. The inverse of the matrix
D\Phi_{(i,k)}
is denoted by
\left(D\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)^{-1}
, in contrast to
D\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
, which is the gradient of the inverse map
\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}
.

Corollary 5.10

(
Deformation bounds).

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}
,
q\geq 0
, and
2\leq N\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
, we have the following bounds on the support of
\psi_{i,q}(x,t){\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}(t)}
.

(5.19a)

\displaystyle\left\|D\Phi_{(i,k)}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}

(5.19b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\left(N-1,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)

(5.19c)

\displaystyle\left\|(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}-{\rm Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}

(5.19d)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N-1}\left((D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\left(N-1,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)

(5.19e)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}\Phi^{-1}_{(i,k)}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\left(N-1,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)

Furthermore, we have the following bounds for
1\leq N+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
:

(5.19f)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}+1}\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(5.19g)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N-N^{\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}D^{N^{\prime}}(D\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

for all
0\leq N^{\prime}\leq N
.

5.3.
Stress estimates and stress cutoff functions

Before giving the definition of the stress cutoffs, we first note that we can upgrade the
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
bounds for
\psi_{i,q-1}D^{K}D_{t,q-1}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
available in (
4.6a
) and (
4.6b
), respectively, to
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
bounds for
\psi_{i,q}D^{K}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
. We claim that:

Lemma 5.11

(
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
estimates for zeroth order stress).

Let
\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
be as defined in (
4.2
). For
q\geq 1
and
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q)
we have the estimates

(5.20a)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{K}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\delta_{q+1}\mathcal{M}\left(K,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q},\tilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(5.20b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{K}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\mathcal{M}\left(K,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q},\tilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
K+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.11
.

The estimate in (
5.20a
) parallels that of
[
5
, Lemma 6.28]
; the ingredients in the proof were the
L^{1}
bounds for the mollified stress, which are available from (
4.6a
) (see also
[
5
, Lemma 5.1]
), and two lemmas regarding sums and iterates of operators. For the sake of clarity, we thus focus on the proof of (
5.20b
), which follows the same strategy as the original proof of (
5.20a
). The only change is that we simply substitute the
L^{\infty}
bound furnished by (
4.6b
) for each instance of an
L^{1}
bound in the proof.

The first step is to apply
[
5
, Lemma A.14 and Remark A.15]
to the functions
v=v_{\ell_{q-1}}
,
f=\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
, with
p=\infty
, and on the domain
\Omega=\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q-1})
. The bound
[
5
, equation (A.50)]
holds in view of the inductive assumption (
2.19
) with
q^{\prime}=q-1
, for the parameters
\mathcal{C}_{v}=\Gamma_{q}^{i+1}\delta_{q-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
,
\lambda_{v}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q-1}
,
\mu_{v}=\Gamma_{q}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{v}=\Gamma_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}
,
N_{x}=2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, and for
N_{*}=\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
. On the other hand, the assumption
[
5
, equation (A.51)]
holds due to (
4.6b
), with the parameters
\mathcal{C}_{f}=\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}
,
\lambda_{f}=\lambda_{q}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{f}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
,
N_{x}=2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
,
\mu_{f}=\Gamma_{q}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{f}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, and
N_{\circ}=2\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
. We thus conclude from
[
5
, equation (A.54)]
that

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}D_{t,q-1}^{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q-1}))}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\mathcal{M}\left(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)

whenever
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
. Here we have used that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{q-1}\leq\lambda_{q}
and that
\Gamma_{q}^{i+1}\delta_{q-1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q-1}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\leq\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}
(in view of (
8.30
), (
8.32
), and (
2.13
)). In particular, the definitions of
\psi_{i,q}
in (
5.7
) and of
\psi_{m,i_{m},q}
in (
5.6
) imply that for all
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
,

(5.21)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}D_{t,q-1}^{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}))}\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\mathcal{M}\left(|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|,2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},\lambda_{q},\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The second step is to apply
[
5
, Lemma A.10]
with
B=D_{t,q-1}
,
A=u_{q}\cdot\nabla
,
v=u_{q}
,
f=\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
,
p=\infty
, and
\Omega=\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})
. In this case
D^{K}(A+B)^{M}f=D^{K}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
, which is exactly the object that we need to estimate in (
5.20b
). The assumption
[
5
, equation (A.40)]
holds due to (
2.18
) at level
q
(which holds due to Proposition
5.6
) with
\mathcal{C}_{v}=\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
,
\lambda_{v}=\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{v}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
,
N_{x}=2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
,
\mu_{v}=\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{v}=\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, and
N_{*}=\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
. The assumption
[
5
, equation (A.41)]
holds due to (
5.21
) with the parameters
\mathcal{C}_{f}=\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}
,
\lambda_{f}=\lambda_{q}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{f}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
,
N_{x}=2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
,
\mu_{f}=\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\mu}_{f}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, and
N_{*}=\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
. The bound
[
5
, equation (A.44)]
and the parameter inequalities
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}-2}\tau_{q}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}
and
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+3}\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}
(which hold due to (
8.31
), (
8.30
), (
8.32
), and (
2.13
)) then directly imply (
5.20b
), concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 5.12

(
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
estimates for higher order stresses).

In order to verify the inductive assumptions in (
2.10a
) and (
2.10b
) for the new stress
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
, it will be necessary to consider a sequence of intermediate objects
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
indexed by
n
for
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
. For notational convenience, when
n=0
, we define
\mathring{R}_{q,0}:=\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
, and estimates on
\mathring{R}_{q,0}
are already provided by Lemma
5.11
. For
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the higher order stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
are defined in Section
7.1
, specifically in (
7.1
). Note that the definition of
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
is given as a finite sum of sub-objects
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
for
n^{\prime}\leq n-1
and thus requires induction on
n
. The definition of
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
is contained in Section
7.3
, specifically in (
7.21
). Estimates on
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
on the support of
\psi_{i,q}
are stated in (
6.13a
) and (
6.13b
) and proven in Section
7.4
. For the time being, we
assume
that
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
is well-defined and satisfies

(5.22)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q,n}\bigr\|_{L^{1}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,n}\lambda_{q,n}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(5.23)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q,n}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\lambda_{q,n}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for
k+m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
.

For the purpose of defining the stress cutoff functions, the precise definitions of the
n
-dependent parameters
\delta_{q+1,n},\lambda_{q,n}
,
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
, and
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
present in (
5.22
) are not relevant. Note however that the definition for
\lambda_{q,n}
for
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
is given in (
2.7a
). Similarly, for
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,
\delta_{q+1,n}
is defined in (
2.7c
). Finally, note that there are losses in the sharpness and order of the available derivative estimates in (
5.22
) and (
5.23
) relative to (
5.20a
) and (
5.20b
). Specifically, the higher order estimates will only be proven up to
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
, which is a parameter that is decreasing with respect to
n
and defined in (
8.29
). For the moment it is only important to note that
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}\gg 14\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
for all
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, which is necessary in order to establish (
2.9a
) and (
2.10a
) at level
q+1
. Similarly, there is a loss in the cost of sharp material derivatives in (
5.22
), as
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
will be a parameter which is decreasing with respect to
n
. When
n=0
, we set
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}=\mathsf{c_{0}}
so that (
5.20a
) is consistent with (
5.22
). For
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
is defined in (
8.27
).

For
q\geq 1
,
0\leq i\leq i_{\rm max}
, and
0\leq n\leq n_{\rm max}
, we keep in mind the bound (
5.22
) and define

(5.24)

\displaystyle g_{i,q,n}^{2}(x,t)=1+\sum_{k=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}}\sum_{m=0}^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\delta_{q+1,n}^{-2}(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n})^{-2k}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+2}\tau_{q}^{-1})^{-2m}|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q,n}(x,t)|^{2}\,.

With this notation, for
j\geq 1
the stress cut-off functions are defined by

(5.25)

\displaystyle\omega_{i,j,q,n}(x,t)=\Psi_{0,q+1}\Big(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2j}\,g_{i,q,n}(x,t)\Big)\,,

while for
j=0
we let

(5.26)

\displaystyle\omega_{i,0,q,n}(x,t)=\widetilde{\Psi}_{0,q+1}\Big(g_{i,q,n}(x,t)\Big)\,,

where
\Psi_{0,q+1}
and
\widetilde{\Psi}_{0,q+1}
are as in Lemma
5.1
.
The cutoff functions
\omega_{i,j,q,n}
defined above will be shown to obey good estimates on the support of the velocity cutoffs
\psi_{i,q}
. An immediate consequence of (
5.1
) with
m=0
is that for every fixed
i,n
, we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq 0}\omega_{i,j,q,n}^{2}=1

on
\mathbb{T}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
. Thus,
\{\omega_{i,j,q,n}^{2}\}_{j\geq 0}
is a partition of unity.

The following Corollary is quite similar to
[
5
, Corollary 6.34]
. In fact the method of proof of that Corollary applies
mutatis mutandis
after replacing each instance of
\mathring{R}_{q,n,p}
and
\lambda_{q,n,p}
with
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
and
\lambda_{q,n}
, and so we omit the proof.

Corollary 5.13

(
L^{\infty}
estimates for the higher order stresses).

For
q\geq 0
,
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, and
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{k}
we have

(5.28)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}D_{t,q}^{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}\mathring{R}_{q,n}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n})}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{2(j+1)}\delta_{q+1,n}(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n})^{|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|}\mathcal{M}\left(|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+2}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
|{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}-4
.

The next Lemma provides an estimate on the maximum value of
j
for which
\psi_{i,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n}
may be non-zero. While the proof is similar in spirit to
[
5
, Lemma 6.35]
, we include the proof since propagating sharp
L^{\infty}
estimates of the stress is one of the crucial new ideas in this paper.

Lemma 5.14

(
Maximal
j
index in the stress cutoffs).

Fix
q\geq 0
and
0\leq n\leq n_{\rm max}
. There exists a
{j_{\rm max}}={j_{\rm max}}(q,n)\geq 1
, which is bounded as

(5.29)

\displaystyle{j_{\rm max}}(q,n)\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(2+\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+3}{b}+\frac{2\beta b^{2}}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}\right)\,,

such that for any
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q)
, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{i,q}\,\omega_{i,j,q,n}\equiv 0\qquad\mbox{for all}\qquad j>j_{\rm max}.

Moreover, assuming that
a=\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large, we have the bound

(5.30)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j_{\rm max}(q,n)}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\delta_{q+1,n}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)+3}\,.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

We define
j_{\rm max}
by

(5.31)

\displaystyle{j_{\rm max}}={j_{\rm max}}(q,n)=\frac{1}{2}\left\lceil\frac{\log(\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\delta_{q+1,n}^{-1})\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)+2}}{\log(\Gamma_{q+1})}\right\rceil.

To see that
j_{\rm max}
may be bounded independently of
q
and
n
, we note that
\delta_{q+1,n}^{-1}\leq\delta_{q+2}^{-1}
, and thus

\displaystyle 2j_{\rm max}\leq 1+\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{b}+\frac{\log(\delta_{q+2}^{-1})}{\log(\Gamma_{q+1})}+14\Upsilon(n)+2\to 3+\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{b}+\frac{2\beta b^{2}}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}+14\Upsilon(n)\quad\mbox{as}\quad q\to\infty\,.

Thus, assuming that
a=\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large, we obtain that

(5.32)

\displaystyle 2{j_{\rm max}}(q,n)\leq 4+\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{b}+\frac{2\beta b^{2}}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}+14\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}})

for all
q\geq 0
and
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
.

To conclude the proof of the Lemma, let
j>j_{\rm max}
, as defined in (
5.31
), and assume by contradiction that there exists a point
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n})\neq\emptyset
. In particular,
j\geq 1
. Then, by (
5.24
)–(
5.25
) and the pigeonhole principle, we see that there exists
0\leq k\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
and
0\leq m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
such that

\displaystyle|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q,n}(x,t)|\geq\frac{\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j}}{\sqrt{8\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}}\delta_{q+1,n}(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n})^{k}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+2}\tau_{q}^{-1})^{m}.

On the other hand, from (
5.20b
) and (
5.23
), we have that

\displaystyle|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q,n}(x,t)|\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n}^{k}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}}\tau_{q}^{-1})^{m}.

The above two estimates imply that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\sqrt{8\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}}\delta_{q+1,n}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\delta_{q+1,n}^{-1},

which contradicts the fact that
j>j_{\rm max}
, as defined in (
5.31
).
∎

The following two lemmas correspond to
[
5
, Lemmas 6.36 and 6.38]
, respectively. As with Corollary
5.13
, the method of proof applies
mutatis mutandis
after dropping the unnecessary subscript
p
. We therefore refer the reader to
[
5
]
for further details.

Lemma 5.15

(
Derivative bounds for the stress cutoffs).

For
q\geq 0
,
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
, and
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, we have that

(5.33)

\displaystyle\frac{{\bf 1}_{\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}}|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\omega_{i,j,q,n}|}{\omega_{i,j,q,n}^{1-(N+M)/\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}\lesssim(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n})^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+3}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\tilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-4
.

Lemma 5.16

(
L^{r}
norm of the stress cutoffs).

Let
q\geq 0
and define
\psi_{i\pm,q}=\left(\psi_{i-1,q}^{2}+\psi_{i,q}^{2}+\psi_{i+1,q}^{2}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
. Then for
r\geq 1
we have that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left\|\omega_{i,j,q,n}\right\|_{L^{r}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i\pm,q}\right)}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{-\nicefrac{{2j}}{{r}}}

holds for all
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, and
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
. The implicit constant is independent of
i,j,q,n
.

5.4.
Anisotropic checkerboard cutoff functions

We construct anisotropic checkerboard cutoff functions which are well-suited for intermittent pipe flows with axes parallel to
e_{3}
. The construction for general
\xi\in\Xi
follows by rotation. Consider a partition of
\mathbb{T}^{3}
into the rectangular prisms defined using

(5.35)

\left\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{T}^{3}\,:\,0\leq x_{1},x_{2}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\Gamma_{q+1}\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}\right)^{-1},\,0\leq x_{3}\leq 2\pi\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}\right\}

and its translations by

\bigl(l_{1}\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\Gamma_{q+1}(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1},l_{2}\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\Gamma_{q+1}(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1},l_{3}2\pi\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}\bigr)\,

for

l_{1},l_{2}\in\{0,\dots,\nicefrac{{\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}}}{{2\pi}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}-1\}\,,\qquad l_{3}\in\{0,\dots,\lambda_{q,n}-1\}\,,

where
\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\geq 1
ensures that the prisms evenly partition
[-\pi,\pi]^{3}
and is bounded above independently of
q
. Index these prisms by integer triples
\vec{l}=(l_{1},l_{2},l_{3})
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{q,n,e_{3},\vec{l}}
be a
C^{\infty}
partition of unity adapted to this checkerboard of anisotropic rectangular prisms which satisfies

(5.36)

\sum_{\vec{l}}\bigl(\mathcal{X}_{q,n,e_{3},\vec{l}}\bigr)^{2}=1

for any
q
and
n
. Specifically, we impose that spatial derivatives applied to cutoffs belonging to this partition of unity cost
\approx\Gamma_{q+1}(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
in the
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
directions, and
\approx\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}
in the
x_{3}
direction, so that

\bigl\|\partial_{1}^{M_{1}}\partial_{2}^{M_{2}}\partial_{3}^{M}\mathcal{X}_{q,n,e_{3},\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)^{M_{1}+M_{2}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{M}\,

for
M_{1},M_{2},M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
. Furthermore, for
\vec{l},\vec{l}^{*}
such that

|l_{1}-l_{1}^{*}|\geq 2,\qquad|l_{2}-l_{2}^{*}|\geq 2,\qquad|l_{3}-l_{3}^{*}|\geq 2,

we impose that

\mathcal{X}_{q,n,e_{3},\vec{l}}\;\mathcal{X}_{q,n,e_{3},\vec{l}^{*}}=0\,.

Incorporating rotations into the above construction, we may similarly produce cutoff functions
\mathcal{X}_{q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
satisfying analogous properties for
\xi\in\Xi
. Note that if
\{\xi,\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime}\}
forms an orthonormal basis for
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then

(5.37)

\bigl\|\left(\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla\right)^{M_{1}}\left(\xi^{\prime\prime}\cdot\nabla\right)^{M_{2}}(\xi\cdot\nabla)^{M}\mathcal{X}_{q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)^{M_{1}+M_{2}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{M}\,.

Definition 5.17

(
Anisotropic checkerboard cutoff function).

Given
q
,
\xi\in\Xi
,
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,
i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
, and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

(5.38)

\zeta_{q,i,k,n,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)=\mathcal{X}_{q,n,{\xi},\vec{l}}\left(\Phi_{i,k,q}(x,t)\right).

These cutoff functions satisfy properties which we enumerate in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.18
.

The cutoff functions
\{\zeta_{q,i,k,n,{\xi},\vec{l}}\}_{\vec{l}}
satisfy the following properties:

(1)

The material derivative
D_{t,q}(\zeta_{q,i,k,n,{\xi},\vec{l}})
vanishes.

(2)

For each
t\in\mathbb{R}
and all
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{T}^{3}
,

(5.39)

\sum_{\vec{l}}\bigl(\zeta_{q,i,k,n,{\xi},\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\bigr)^{2}=1\,.

(3)

Let
A=(\nabla\Phi_{i,k,q})^{-1}
. Then we have the spatial derivative estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N_{1}}D_{t,q}^{M}({\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}})^{N_{2}}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}\right)^{N_{1}}\lambda_{q,n}^{N_{2}}

(5.40)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{0}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

for all
N_{1}+N_{2}+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}+1
.

(4)

There exists an implicit dimensional constant
\mathcal{C}_{\chi}
independent of
q
,
n
,
k
,
i
, and
\vec{l}
such that for all
(x,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
, the support of
\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(\cdot,t)
satisfies

(5.41)

\textnormal{diam}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(\cdot,t)))\lesssim\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}\,.

Proof of Lemma
5.18
.

The proof of (
1
) is immediate from (
5.38
). (
5.39
) follows from (
1
) and (
5.36
). To verify (
3
), the only nontrivial calculations are those including the differential operator
(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j})
. Using the Leibniz rule, the contraction

\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}=\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}(\partial_{m}\mathcal{X}_{q,n,\xi,\vec{l}})(\Phi_{i,k,q})\partial_{j}\Phi_{i,k,q}^{m}=\xi^{m}(\partial_{m}\mathcal{X}_{q,n,\xi,\vec{l}})(\Phi_{i,k,q})\,,

(
5.37
), and (
5.19g
) gives the desired estimate. The proof of (
5.41
) follows from the construction of
\mathcal{X}_{q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
and the Lipschitz bound obeyed by
\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}
on the support of
\psi_{i,q}
; see for example (
3.16
).
∎

5.5.
Definition of the cumulative cutoff function

Finally, combining the cutoff functions defined in Definition
5.5
, (
5.25
)–(
5.26
), and (
5.15
), we define the cumulative cutoff function by

\displaystyle{\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)=\psi_{i,q}(x,t)\omega_{i,j,q,n}(x,t)\chi_{i,k,q}(t)\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)\,.}

Since the values of
q
and
n
are clear from the context and the values of
\xi
and
\vec{l}
are irrelevant in many arguments, we may abbreviate the above using any of

\displaystyle\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,(x,t)=\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi}(x,t)=\eta_{(i,j,k)}(x,t)=\psi_{(i)}(x,t)\omega_{(i,j)}(x,t)\chi_{(i,k)}(t)\zeta_{(i,k)}(x,t)\,.

It follows from (
2.11
) at level
q
, (
5.27
), (
5.13
), and (
5.39
) that for every
{(q,n,\xi)}
fixed, we have

(5.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\vec{l}}\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,^{2}(x,t)=1\,.

The sum in
i
goes up to
i_{\rm max}
(defined in (
5.12
)), while the sum in
j
goes up to
j_{\rm max}
(defined in (
5.31
)).

We conclude this section with support estimates on the cumulative cutoff functions
\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
.

Lemma 5.19
.

For
r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with
\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}=1
and any
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}
,
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}
, and
\xi\in\Xi
, we have that

(5.43)

\sum_{\vec{l}}\left|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}})\right|\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{\frac{-2i+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}{r_{1}}+\frac{-2j}{r_{2}}+2}\,.

Proof of Lemma
5.19
.

From (
2.17
) at level
q
and (
5.34
), we have that for each fixed time
t
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q})\cap\mathrm{supp\,}(\omega_{i,j,q,n})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(\psi_{i-1,q}^{2}+\psi_{i,q}^{2}+\psi_{i+1,q}^{2}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\left(\omega_{i,j-1,q,n}^{2}+\omega_{i,j,q,n}^{2}+\omega_{i,j+1,q,n}^{2}\right)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{\frac{-2(i-1)+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}{r_{1}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{\frac{-2(j-1)}{r_{2}}}\,.

Using the fact that
\{\eta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\}_{\vec{l}}
forms a partition of unity from (
5.39
) and
\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}=1
gives the desired estimate.
∎

6.
Inductive propositions

6.1.
Induction on
q

The main claim of this section is an induction on
q
. Notice that the estimates in this proposition match the inductive assumptions (
2.9
) and (
2.10
) at level
q+1
.

Proposition 6.1

(
Inductive Step on
q
)
.

Given the velocity field
v_{\ell_{q}}
which solves the Euler-Reynolds system with stress
\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}+\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}
, where
v_{\ell_{q}}
,
\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
, and
\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}
satisfy the conclusions of Lemma
4.1
in addition to (
2.8a
)–(
2.21b
), there exist
v_{q+1}=v_{\ell_{q}}+w_{q+1}
and
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
which satisfy the following:

(1)

v_{q+1}
solves the Euler-Reynolds system with stress
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
.

(2)

For all
k,m\leq 7\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, we have

(6.1a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}w_{q+1}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-1}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(6.1b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}w_{q+1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\Theta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3)

For all
k,m\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, we have

(6.2a)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q+1}\bigr\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(6.2b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q+1}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+2}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

6.2.
Notations

The proof of Proposition
6.1
will be achieved through an induction with respect to
\widetilde{n}
, where
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
corresponds to the addition of the perturbation
\displaystyle w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
. We shall employ the notation:

(1)

{\widetilde{n}}
- An integer taking values
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
over which induction is performed, indexing the component
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
of the velocity increment
w_{q+1}
.
We emphasize that the use of
{\widetilde{n}}
at various points in statements and estimates means that we are
currently
working on the inductive step at level
{\widetilde{n}}
.

(2)

n
- An integer taking values
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
which correspond to the higher order stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
. Occasionally, we shall use the notation
\mathring{R}_{q,0}=\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}
to streamline an argument. We emphasize that
n
will be used at various points in statements and estimates to reference
higher order
objects in addition to those at level
{\widetilde{n}}
, and so will satisfy the inequality
{\widetilde{n}}\leq n
.

(3)

{\mathring{H}}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
- The component of
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
originating from an error term produced by the addition of
w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}
. The parameter
n^{\prime}
will always be a
subsidiary
parameter used to reference objects created at or
below
the level
{\widetilde{n}}
that we are currently working on, and so will satisfy
n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}
.

(4)

\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}
- We use the spatial Littlewood-Paley projectors
\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}
defined by

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}_{\left[\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1},\lambda_{q,1}\right)}&\mbox{if }n=1\,,\\
\mathbb{P}_{\left[\lambda_{q,n-1},\lambda_{q,n}\right)}&\mbox{if }2\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}\,,\\
\mathbb{P}_{\geq\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}}&\mbox{if }n={n_{\rm max}}+1\,,\end{cases}

where
\mathbb{P}_{[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})}
is defined in Remark
3.4
as
\mathbb{P}_{\geq\lambda_{1}}\mathbb{P}_{<\lambda_{2}}
.
Errors which include the frequency projector
\mathbb{P}_{[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1]}
will be small enough to be absorbed into
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
. We note that if
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, then from (
2.7b
), any
\frac{\mathbb{T}^{3}}{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
-periodic function satisfies

(6.4)

\displaystyle f=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f+\mathbb{P}_{\geq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}f=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f+\sum_{n={\widetilde{n}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}f\,.

(5)

In order to later deduce a useful refinement of (
6.4
), we set

(6.5)

\displaystyle r({\widetilde{n}})=\begin{cases}0&\mbox{if }{\widetilde{n}}=0\,,\\
\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+{\widetilde{n}}}{2}&\mbox{if }1\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1\,.\end{cases}

(6)

In order to keep track of small losses related to the process of building a stress
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
, corrector
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
, and new stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
for
n>{\widetilde{n}}
, we define

(6.6)

\displaystyle\Upsilon(n)=\begin{cases}0&\mbox{if }n=0\,,\\
1&\mbox{if }1\leq n\leq\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{2}\\
k&\mbox{if }\frac{2^{k-1}-1}{2^{k-1}}{n_{\rm max}}<n\leq\frac{2^{k}-1}{2^{k}}{n_{\rm max}}\\
2+\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil&\mbox{if }n={n_{\rm max}}\,.\end{cases}

\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})
gives an upper bound on the number of steps in the induction on
{\widetilde{n}}
it takes to produce the
entire
error term
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
. A consequence of (
6.5
) and (
6.6
) is that

(6.7)

\displaystyle n>r({\widetilde{n}})\qquad\implies\qquad\Upsilon(n)\geq\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+1\,.

To prove this, first consider the case
n={n_{\rm max}}
. Then for all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1
, we have that
r({\widetilde{n}})<{n_{\rm max}}
, and so (
6.7
) should hold for all
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
. Since
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
, there exists a minimum value of
k
, say
k_{\widetilde{n}}
, such that
{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{2^{k_{\widetilde{n}}}}
, which implies that
\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})\leq k_{\widetilde{n}}
. For
k=\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil+2
, however, we have that
{n_{\rm max}}-\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{2^{k-1}}\geq{n_{\rm max}}-\frac{1}{2}
, and so it must be the case that
k_{\widetilde{n}}\leq\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil+1
, which proves (
6.7
) in the case
n={n_{\rm max}}
, and shows that

(6.8)

\Upsilon(n)\leq 2+\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil\qquad\forall n\leq{n_{\rm max}}\,.

To prove (
6.7
) in the remaining cases, note that if
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, then
n>r(0)\implies n\geq 1
and so (
6.7
) holds. If
{\widetilde{n}}=1
, then
n>\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+1}{2}
, and again (
6.7
) holds. Finally, if
2\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1
and
\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})=k
, then

\displaystyle n

\displaystyle>\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+{\widetilde{n}}}{2}>\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+\frac{2^{k-1}-1}{2^{k-1}}{{n_{\rm max}}}}{2}=\frac{2^{k}-1}{2^{k}}{n_{\rm max}}\qquad\implies\qquad\Upsilon(n)\geq k+1\,.

(7)

\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
- For any
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1
, this is any stress term which satisfies the estimates required of
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
and which has already been estimated at the
{\widetilde{n}}^{th}
stage of the induction; that is, error terms arising from the addition of
w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}
for
n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}
. We
exclude

\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}
from
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
, only absorbing it at the very end when we define
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
. Thus

(6.9)

\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}+1}=\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}+\left(\textnormal{errors coming from }w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}+1}\textnormal{ that also go into }\mathring{R}_{q+1}\right)\,.

We adopt the convention that
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{-1}=0
.

(8)

We adopt the convention that
\sum_{n=0}^{-1}f(n)\equiv\sum_{n={n_{\rm max}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}}f(n)\equiv 0
denotes an
empty
summation.

6.3.
Induction on
\widetilde{n}

We split the verification of Proposition
6.1
using a sub-inductive procedure on the parameter
{\widetilde{n}}
. Note that summing (
6.11a
)-(
6.12b
) over
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, appealing to (
8.43
) and (
8.48
), and using the extra factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
to kill implicit constants, we have matched the desired bounds in (
6.1a
)-(
6.2b
).

Proposition 6.2

(
Induction on
{\widetilde{n}}
: From
{\widetilde{n}}-1
to
{\widetilde{n}}
for
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
)
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
6.1
and Lemma
4.1
, we let
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
be given, and let

v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}=v_{\ell_{q}}+\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}
,

\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}
, and
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
be given for
0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1
and
{\widetilde{n}}\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, such that the following are satisfied:

(1)

{v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}}
solves the Euler-Reynolds system with stress

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\{{\widetilde{n}}=0\}}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}+\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}+\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\sum\limits_{n>r(n^{\prime})}^{{n_{\rm max}}}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}+\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}\,.

(2)

For all
k+m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{fin},\textnormal{n}^{\prime}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}-9
and
0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1
,

(6.11a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,n^{\prime}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}^{\prime}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(6.11b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon(n^{\prime})+\frac{7}{2}}r_{q+1,n^{\prime}}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}^{\prime}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(3)

For all
k,m\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
and
1\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,

(6.12a)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\bigr\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(6.12b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

(4)

For
0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1
,
r(n^{\prime})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, and all
k+m\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
,

(6.13a)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\bigr\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,n}\lambda_{q,n}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,,

(6.13b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\lambda_{q,n}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

Then if
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1
, there exists
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
, and
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}
for
0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}
, such that (
6.10
)–(
6.13b
) are satisfied with
{\widetilde{n}}-1
replaced with
{\widetilde{n}}
. If
{\widetilde{n}}={n_{\rm max}}
, then there exists
w_{q+1,{n_{\rm max}}}
and
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
such that
v_{q+1}:=v_{q,{n_{\rm max}}-1}+w_{q+1,{n_{\rm max}}}
solves the Euler-Reynolds system with stress
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
, and
v_{q+1}
,
w_{q+1}
, and
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
satisfy conclusions (
6.1a
)–(
6.2b
) from Proposition
6.1
.

7.
Proving the main inductive estimates

7.1.
Definition of
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
and
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}

In this section we define the stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
and the perturbations
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
used to correct them. For
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we define

(7.1)

\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}=\mathbf{1}_{\{{\widetilde{n}}=0\}}\mathring{R}_{\ell_{q}}+\sum_{0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1}\mathring{H}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{n^{\prime}}\,.

In Subsection
7.3
, we will show that
\mathring{H}^{n^{\prime}}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
is zero in certain parameter regimes, although for the moment this is irrelevant.
Now for any fixed values of
{\widetilde{n}}
,
i
,
j
, and
k
, we may define

(7.2)

R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}=\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}\left(\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma^{2j+4}_{q+1}\mathrm{Id}-\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\,.

Let
\xi\in\Xi
be a vector from Proposition
3.1
. For all
\xi\in\Xi
, we define the coefficient function
a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}
by

(7.3)

a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}:=a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}}}:=a_{(\xi)}=\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j+2}_{q+1}\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}\,\gamma_{\xi}\left(\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma^{2j+4}_{q+1}}\right)\,.

From Corollary
5.13
, we see that on the support of
\eta_{(i,j,k)}
we have
|\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}|\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+2}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
, and thus by estimate (
5.19a
) from Corollary
5.10
, we have that

\left|\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma^{2j+4}_{q+1}}-\mathrm{Id}\right|\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}<\frac{1}{2}

once
\lambda_{0}
is sufficiently large. Thus we may apply Proposition
3.1
.

The coefficient function
a_{(\xi)}
is then multiplied by an intermittent pipe flow defined in Proposition
3.3
(with
\lambda=\lambda_{q+1}
and
r=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
)

\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}^{s}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)},

where the superscript
s=s(i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l})
indicates the placement of the intermittent pipe flow
\mathbb{W}^{s}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
(cf. (
2
) from Proposition
3.3
), which depends on
i
,
j
,
k
,
{\widetilde{n}}
, and
\vec{l}
and is only relevant in Section
7.5
. To ease notation, we will suppress the superscript
s
(except in Section
7.5
), and use the shorthand notation

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}:=\mathbb{W}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{s}\,.

We will also adopt the same notational conventions for the potentials
\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
. Furthermore, (
3.8
) from Proposition
3.3
gives that we can now write the principal part of the first term of the perturbation as

(7.5)

w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}=\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\vec{l}}\sum_{\xi}a_{(\xi)}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right):=\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\vec{l}}\sum_{\xi}w_{(\xi)}\,.

The notation
w_{(\xi)}
implicitly encodes all indices and thus will be a useful shorthand for the principal part of the perturbation. To make the perturbation divergence free, we add

(7.6)

w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\vec{l}}\sum_{\xi}\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)=\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\vec{l}}\sum_{\xi}w_{(\xi)}^{(c)}

so that

(7.7)

w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}=w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}=\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\vec{l}}\sum_{\xi}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\,.

7.2.
Estimates for
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}

In this section, we verify (
6.11a
) and (
6.11b
). We first estimate the
L^{r}
norms of the coefficient functions
a_{(\xi)}
. We have consolidated the proofs for each value of
{\widetilde{n}}
into the following lemma.

Lemma 7.1
.

For
N,N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime},M
with
N^{\prime},N^{\prime\prime}\in\{0,1\}
and
N+N^{\prime}+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-4
, and
r,r_{1},r_{2}\in[1,\infty]
with
\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}=1
, we have the following estimate.

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{p}\partial_{p})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{j+2}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}

(7.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right).

In the case that
r=\infty
, the above estimate gives that

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N-N^{\prime\prime}}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{p}\partial_{p})^{N^{\prime}}D^{N^{\prime\prime}}a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+\frac{7}{2}}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

We first compute (
7.8
) for the case
r=\infty
. Recalling estimate (
5.28
), we have that for all
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-4
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{(i,j,k)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma^{2j+2}_{q+1}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+2},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right).

From Corollary
5.10
, we have that for all
N+M\leq\nicefrac{{3\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}}}{{2}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}D\Phi_{(i,k)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}(\psi_{i,q}\chi_{i,k,q}))}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}}\tau_{q}^{-1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right).

Thus from the Leibniz rule and definition (
7.2
), for
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-4
,

(7.10)
