\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{(i,j,k)})}\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma^{2j+4}_{q+1}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+2},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The above estimates allow us to apply
[
5
, Lemma A.5]
with
N=N^{\prime}
,
M=M^{\prime}
so that
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-4
,
\psi=\gamma_{\xi,}
,

\Gamma_{\psi}=1
,
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
,
D_{t}=D_{t,q}
,
h(x,t)=R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}(x,t)
,
C_{h}=\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}=\Gamma^{2}
,
\lambda=\widetilde{\lambda}=\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
,
\mu=\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+2}
,
\widetilde{\mu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
, and
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
. We obtain that for all
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-4
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\gamma_{\xi}\left(\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}},{\widetilde{p}}}\Gamma^{2j+4}_{q+1}}\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{(i,j,k)})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+2},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

From the above bound, definition (
7.3
), the Leibniz rule, estimate (
2.23
) at level
q
in conjunction with (
8.35
), (
5.19g
), (
5.16
), (
5.33
), and (
3
), we obtain that for
N+N^{\prime}+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-4
,

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{p}\partial_{p})^{N^{\prime}}a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{j+2}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})^{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times(\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}})^{N^{\prime}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

Then, using (
5.30
) the above bound becomes (
7.9
) for
N^{\prime\prime}=0
. The proof for
N^{\prime\prime}=1
is nearly identical, and we omit the details. When
r\neq\infty
, we use
\left\|f\right\|_{L^{r}}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{L^{\infty}}|\{\mathrm{supp\,}f\}|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}
and the demonstrated bound for
r=\infty
to obtain (
7.8
) for the full range of
r
.
∎

An immediate consequence of Lemma
7.1
is that we have estimates for the velocity increments themselves. These are summarized in the following corollary. The proofs for
r\neq\infty
are analogous to those from
[
5
, Corollary 8.2]
and therefore use Lemma
A.1
. We only note that the gap between the spatial derivative cost of
a_{(\xi)}
(
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
from Lemma
7.1
) and the minimum frequency of
\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
(
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
from (
7.4
) and Proposition
3.3
) is now only
\Gamma_{q+1}
, and so we need the inequality (
8.37
) in order to satisfy (
A.2
). The assumption (
A.1
) follows from (
8.58a
). The estimates for
r=\infty
follow directly from (
7.9
) and (
3.6
).

Corollary 7.2
.

For
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}-8
and
(r,r_{1},r_{2})\in\left\{(1,2,2),(2,\infty,1)\right\}
, for
w_{(\xi)}
we have the estimates

(7.11a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi)}\right\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j+2}_{q+1}{\left(r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{2}{r}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.11b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi)}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+\frac{7}{2}}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

For
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}-9
and
(r,r_{1},r_{2})\in\left\{(1,2,2),(2,\infty,1)\right\}
, we have that

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi)}^{(c)}\bigr\|_{L^{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{r}}}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma^{j+2}_{q+1}{\left(r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)}^{\frac{2}{r}-1}

(7.12a)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.12b)

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}w_{(\xi)}^{(c)}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}\frac{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+\frac{7}{2}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

Remark 7.3
.

Note that the above estimates verify the bounds (
6.11a
) and (
6.11b
) after summing on
(i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l})
and using (
5.43
) with
r_{1}=\infty
and
r_{2}=2
. Then from (
7.5
)–(
7.7
), (
7.11a
)–(
7.12b
), and the parameter inequalities (
8.25
), (
8.43
), and (
8.48
), the bounds (
6.1a
) and (
6.1b
) follow after using the extra factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
to absorb implicit constants.

7.3.
Identification of error terms

Recall that
v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}
is divergence-free and is a solution to the Euler-Reynolds system with stress given in (
6.10
). Now using the definition of
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
from (
7.1
) for
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we add
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
as defined in (
7.7
), we have that
v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}:=v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}+\mathrm{div\,}\left(v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)+\nabla p_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\right)+\mathrm{div\,}\Biggl(\sum\limits_{n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\sum\limits_{n>r(n^{\prime})}^{{n_{\rm max}}}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\Biggr)+\mathrm{div\,}\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}

\displaystyle\quad+D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}+2\sum_{n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1}\mathrm{div\,}\left(w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}\otimes_{\rm s}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)

(7.13)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{div\,}\left(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right).

Here we use the notation
a\otimes_{\rm s}b=\frac{1}{2}(a\otimes b+b\otimes a)
.
The first term on the right hand side is
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}
, which for
{\widetilde{n}}\geq 1
satisfies the same estimates as
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
by (
6.12a
) and will thus be absorbed into
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
. The second term, save for the fact that the sum is over
n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1
rather than
n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}
and is therefore missing the terms
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{\widetilde{n}}
, matches (
6.10
) at level
{\widetilde{n}}
(i.e. replacing every instance of
{\widetilde{n}}-1
with
{\widetilde{n}}
). We apply the inverse divergence operators from Proposition
A.2
to the transport and Nash errors to obtain

D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}=\mathrm{div\,}\left((\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}\right)\right)+\nabla\pi,

and these errors are absorbed into
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
or the new pressure. We will show in Section
7.5
that the interaction of
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
with previous terms
w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}
is a Type 2 oscillation error so that

(7.14)

2\sum_{0\leq n^{\prime}\leq{\widetilde{n}}-1}w_{q+1,n^{\prime}}\otimes_{\rm s}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}=0\,.

So to verify (
6.10
) at level
{\widetilde{n}}
, only the analysis of last line of the right-hand side of (
7.13
) remains.

For a fixed
{\widetilde{n}}
, throughout this section we will consider sums over indices
(\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l})
, where the direction vector
\xi
takes on one of the finitely many values in Proposition
3.3
,
0\leq i\leq{i_{\rm max}}(q)
indexes the velocity cutoffs,
0\leq j\leq{j_{\rm max}}(q,{\widetilde{n}})
indexes the stress cutoffs, the parameter
k\in\mathbb{Z}
indexes the time cutoffs defined in (
5.15
), and lastly,
\vec{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{3}_{0}
indexes the checkerboard cutoffs from Definition
5.17
. For brevity of notation, we denote sums over such indexes as

\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\,.

Moreover, we shall denote as

\sum_{\neq\{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}\}}

the
double-summation
over indexes
(\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l})
and
({\xi^{*}},{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},\vec{l}^{*})
which belong to the set

\left\{(\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l})\,,({\xi^{*}},{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}):\xi\neq{\xi^{*}}\lor i\neq{i^{*}}\lor j\neq{j^{*}}\lor k\neq{k^{*}}\lor\vec{l}\neq\vec{l}^{*}\right\}\,.

We may now write out the self-interaction of
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{div\,}\left(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathrm{curl\,}\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\bigr)\otimes\mathrm{curl\,}\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{i,k}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\neq\{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}\}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathrm{curl\,}\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\bigr)\otimes\mathrm{curl\,}\bigl(a_{({\xi^{*}})}\nabla\Phi_{({i^{*}},{k^{*}})}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{{\xi^{*}},q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\bigr)\right)

(7.15)

\displaystyle=:\mathrm{div\,}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1}+\mathrm{div\,}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},2}.

We will show that
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},2}
is a Type 2 oscillation error so that

(7.16)

\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},2}=0\,.

Splitting
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1}
gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{div\,}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\otimes\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\otimes_{\rm s}\bigl(\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\bigr)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(\bigl(\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\bigr)\otimes\bigl(\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\bigl(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)\bigr)\right)

(7.17)

\displaystyle:=\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,1}+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}\right).

The last two of these terms are divergence corrector errors and will therefore be absorbed into
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
and estimated in Section
7.6
. So the only terms which we have yet to identify from (
7.13
) are
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,1}
and
\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
.

Recall cf. (
7.4
) that
\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
is periodized to scale
\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{-1}
. Using (
6.4
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$ }}\kern-7.83337pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$ }}\kern-6.11674pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.48965pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$ }}\kern-5.31259pt}}\!\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}{\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}+\sum_{n={\widetilde{n}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)\,.

Using (
4
) and (
3.9
) from Proposition
3.3
in combination with the above identity, and the convention that
\bullet
denotes the unspecified components of a vector field, we then split
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,1}
as

\displaystyle\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,1}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\left(a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathrm{div\,}\bigg(a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\sum_{n={\widetilde{n}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}\otimes\mathbb{W})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div\,}\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}\eta_{(i,j,k)}^{2}\gamma_{\xi}^{2}\left(\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\sum_{n={\widetilde{n}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}\otimes\mathbb{W})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

(7.18)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\sum_{n={\widetilde{n}}+1}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}^{\gamma})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\,.

By (
3.1
) from Proposition
3.1
, identity (
7.2
), and (
5.39
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k,\xi}\sum_{\vec{l}}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}^{2}\,\gamma_{\xi}^{2}\left(\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i,j,k,\xi}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}\psi_{i,q}^{2}\omega_{i,j,q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\chi_{i,k,q}^{2}\gamma_{\xi}^{2}\left(\frac{R_{q,{\widetilde{n}},j,i,k}}{\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}}\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\left(\xi\otimes\xi\right)\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i,j,k}\psi_{i,q}^{2}\omega_{i,j,q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\chi_{i,k,q}^{2}\left(\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}\mathrm{Id}-\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)

(7.19)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}+\mathrm{Id}\biggl(\sum_{\;i,j,k}\psi_{i,q}^{2}\omega_{i,j,q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\chi_{i,k,q}^{2}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+4}\biggr)\,,

where in the last equality we have appealed to the fact that
\eta^{2}_{i,j,k}
forms a partition of unity, cf. (
5.42
). The second term on the right hand side of (
7.19
) is a pressure term.

Returning to the second and third lines in (
7.18
), we first note that when
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, (
2.7b
) gives that
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,0}=\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q,n}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}=\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
. Then from (
6.3
) and (
7.4
), for all
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}+1
, we deduce that
\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,0}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,0}\right)\neq 0
. Conversely, when
1\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, for all
n\geq{\widetilde{n}}+1
such that
\lambda_{q,n}<\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}
,
i.e. such that the maximal frequency of
\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}
is less than the minimal frequency of
\mathbb{P}_{\neq 0}\left(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)
, we have that
\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)=0
. Using (
2.7a
), we write that

\displaystyle\qquad\underbrace{\lambda_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}}_{=\lambda_{q,n}}<\underbrace{\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{q}^{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{{\widetilde{n}}}{4({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{{\widetilde{n}}}{4({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}}_{=\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad\lambda_{q}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{{\widetilde{n}}-2n}{4({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}<\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{{\widetilde{n}}-2n}{4({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad 2\varepsilon_{\Gamma}<\frac{1}{4}+\frac{{\widetilde{n}}-2n}{4({n_{\rm max}}+1)}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\qquad 8\varepsilon_{\Gamma}({n_{\rm max}}+1)<{n_{\rm max}}+1+{\widetilde{n}}-2n

(7.20)

\displaystyle\Leftarrow

\displaystyle\qquad n\leq\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+{\widetilde{n}}}{2}\,,\qquad\frac{1}{2}-4\varepsilon_{\Gamma}({n_{\rm max}}+1)>0\,.

The second inequality in the last line follows from (
8.6a
). Based on (
7.20
) and (
6.5
), we apply Proposition
A.2
in the parameter regimes
{\widetilde{n}}=0,1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and
1\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}-1,r({\widetilde{n}})=\frac{{n_{\rm max}}+{\widetilde{n}}}{2}<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
to define

\displaystyle\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{\widetilde{n}}:=\mathcal{H}\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

(7.21)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\gamma})(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\bigg)\,.

The terms from (
7.18
) with
\mathbb{P}_{[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1]}
will be absorbed into
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
. We will show shortly that the terms
\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{\widetilde{n}}
in (
7.21
) are precisely the terms needed to make (
7.13
) match (
6.10
) at level
{\widetilde{n}}
.

Recall from (
6.9
) that
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{\widetilde{n}}
will include
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}
in addition to error terms arising from the addition of
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
which are small enough to be absorbed in
\mathring{R}_{q+1}
. Then to check (
6.10
), we return to (
7.13
) and use (
7.14
), (
7.15
), (
7.17
), (
7.18
), (
7.19
), (
7.20
), (
6.5
), and (
7.21
) to write

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}+\mathrm{div\,}\left(v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes v_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)+\nabla p_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div\,}\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}+\mathrm{div\,}\biggl(\sum\limits_{\;n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\sum\limits_{n>r(n^{\prime})}^{{n_{\rm max}}}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\biggr)+\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}+\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}\right)+\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,1}+\mathring{R}_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div\,}\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}+\mathrm{div\,}\biggl(\sum\limits_{\;n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}\sum\limits_{n>r(n^{\prime})}^{{n_{\rm max}}}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{div\,}\bigg(\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}+(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}\bigg)+\nabla\pi

\displaystyle\quad+\mathrm{div\,}\bigg[\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\sum_{n>r({\widetilde{n}})}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}\otimes\mathbb{W})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}\bigg)

(7.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\sum_{n>r({\widetilde{n}})}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}^{\gamma})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\bigg)\bigg]

(7.23)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{div\,}\mathring{R}_{q}^{\textnormal{comm}}+\mathrm{div\,}\biggl(\sum\limits_{\;n^{\prime}=0}^{{\widetilde{n}}}\sum\limits_{n>r(n^{\prime})}^{{n_{\rm max}}}\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{n^{\prime}}\biggr)+\mathrm{div\,}\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}+\nabla\pi\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}

\displaystyle=\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}-1}+(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}+\pi\mathrm{Id}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{H}\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1]}(\mathbb{W}\otimes\mathbb{W})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{H}\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1]}(\mathbb{W}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}^{\gamma})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{R}^{*}\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\sum_{n>r({\widetilde{n}})}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}\otimes\mathbb{W})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}\bigg)

(7.24)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{R}^{*}\bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\sum_{n>r({\widetilde{n}})}^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}^{\gamma})_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\bigg)\,.

We first emphasize that to obtain (
7.23
), we have used that the Type 2 oscillation errors from (
7.14
) and (
7.16
) will be shown to vanish. In addition, the symmetric stress
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}
will be defined in The equality (
7.23
) completes the proof of (
6.10
) at level
{\widetilde{n}}
.

7.4.
Type 1 oscillation errors

Recall from (
7.23
) that there are two main categories of Type 1 oscillation errors which arise from the addition of
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
: the higher order stresses
H_{q,n}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
, which are defined and non-vanishing in (
7.21
) in the parameter regimes
{\widetilde{n}}=0,1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and
1\leq{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}},r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, and the portions of
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
, which are defined in the last four lines of (
7.24
). To estimate these error terms, we will first analyze a single term of the form

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)\Bigg(\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\nabla a_{(\xi)}^{2}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})(\Phi_{(i,k)})\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-T}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}^{2}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\theta}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\gamma})(\Phi_{(i,k)})\partial_{\alpha}(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\Bigg)

(7.25)

\displaystyle\qquad=:\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}+\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}^{*}\,,

where
\bullet
refers to the unspecified components of a vector field, and superscripts on
\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
refer to components of vectors over which summation is performed. In the above display, we allow
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}+1
, thus including both
H_{q,n}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
from (
7.21
) and all Type 1 error terms in (
7.24
).

Lemma 7.4
.

The terms
\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}^{*}
defined in (
7.25
) satisfy the following estimates.

(1)

For all error terms
\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}^{*}
, which are the outputs of
\mathcal{R}^{*}
, we have for all
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
that

(7.26)

\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}^{*}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq{\delta_{q+2}}\lambda_{q+1}^{N-1}\tau_{q}^{-M}\,.

(2)

For
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and
n={n_{\rm max}}+1
, the high frequency, local part of the Type 1 errors satisfies

(7.27a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{{n_{\rm max}}+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(7.27b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{{n_{\rm max}}+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
.

(3)

For
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
and
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the medium frequency, local part of the Type 1 errors satisfies

(7.28a)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,n}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(7.28b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
N+M\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
.

Remark 7.5
.

In order to verify (
6.13a
) for
n^{\prime}={\widetilde{n}}
and
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we first note that
\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}=\mathring{H}_{q,n}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
, and the inequality
\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}}\,,
holds from
{\widetilde{n}}\leq n-1
and (
8.27
). Then (
7.28a
) provides the desired bound. (
6.13b
) follows similarly from (
7.28b
). The bound in (
6.12a
) follows from (
7.26
) and (
7.27a
), since
\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}\geq 4
from (
8.5
) and (
8.27
). The bound in (
6.12b
) follows from (
7.26
) and (
7.27b
). Lastly, when
{\widetilde{n}}={n_{\rm max}}
, and hence
n={n_{\rm max}}+1
, (
7.26
), (
7.27a
), and (
7.27b
) match (
6.2a
) and (
6.2b
).

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

We use (
1
) from Proposition
3.3
and the notation
A=(\nabla\Phi)^{-1}
to rewrite (
7.25
) as

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)\biggl(\sum_{\;\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\left(\varrho_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{2}\right)(\Phi_{(i,k)})\xi^{\theta}\xi^{\gamma}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\theta}^{\alpha}\left(\partial_{\alpha}a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}+a_{(\xi)}^{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\right)\biggr)\,.

Next, we must identify the functions and the values of the parameters which will be used in the application of Proposition
A.2
. We first address the bounds required in (
A.4
), (
A.5
), and (
A.6
), which we can treat simultaneously for items (
1
), (
2
), and (
3
). Afterwards, we split the proof into two parts. First, we set
n={n_{\rm max}}+1
and prove (
7.26
), (
7.27a
), and (
7.27b
) for any value of
{\widetilde{n}}
. Next, we consider
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
and
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and prove (
7.26
) in the remaining cases, as we simultaneously prove (
7.28a
) and (
7.28b
).

Returning to (
A.4
), we will verify that this inequality holds with
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
,
D_{t}=D_{t,q}=\partial_{t}+v_{\ell_{q}}\cdot\nabla
, and
\displaystyle N_{*}=M_{*}=\lfloor\nicefrac{{N^{\sharp}}}{{2}}\rfloor
, where
N^{\sharp}=\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5
. In order to verify the assumption
N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}\geq 2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4
, we use that
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
and
{\mathsf{d}}
satisfy (
8.58a
). We fix values of
(i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l})
and set

(7.29)

\displaystyle G^{\bullet}=\xi^{\theta}\xi^{\gamma}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\theta}^{\alpha}\left(\partial_{\alpha}a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}+a_{(\xi)}^{2}\partial_{\alpha}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\right)\,.

Note crucially that the differential operator falling on
a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}^{2}
in the first term is precisely
\xi^{\theta}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\theta}^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}
, which from (
3
) and (
7.8
) will obey a good bound. We now establish (
A.4
)–(
A.6
) with the parameter choices

(7.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,1}

\displaystyle=|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+5}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}=\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+8}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\,,

\lambda=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
,
M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
,
\nu=\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}
,
\widetilde{\nu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
, and
\lambda^{\prime}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
.

To establish an
L^{1}
bound for the first term from (
7.29
), we appeal to Lemma
7.1
, estimate (
7.8
) with
N^{\prime}=1
, and (
5.19g
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\Biggl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\xi^{\theta}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\theta}^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\xi^{\gamma}\right)\Biggr\|_{L^{1}}

(7.31)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2j+5}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

holds for all
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor
. It is precisely at this point that we have used that the differential operator
\xi^{\theta}(A_{(i,k)})_{\theta}^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}
costs only
\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
. For the
L^{\infty}
bound on the same term, we argue similarly except we apply estimate (
7.9
) to obtain

\displaystyle\Biggl\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\xi^{\theta}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\theta}^{\alpha}\partial_{\alpha}a_{(\xi)}^{2}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\xi^{\gamma}\right)\Biggr\|_{L^{\infty}}

(7.32)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+8}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

For the second term from (
7.29
), we can appeal to (
5.19g
) and use that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
for all
{\widetilde{n}}
to deduce that for
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\partial_{\alpha}\left(A_{(i,k)}\right)_{\gamma}^{\bullet}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q})}\lesssim\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{N+1}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c_{0}}+1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right).

Combining this with Lemma
7.1
, estimate (
7.8
) in the case
p=1
and (
7.9
) in the case
p=\infty
produces identical bounds as for the first term and in the range

N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor
. Adding both estimates together shows that (
A.4
) has been satisfied for both
p=1,\infty
.

We set the flow in Proposition
A.2
as
\Phi=\Phi_{i,k}
, which by definition satisfies
D_{t,q}\Phi_{i,k}=0
. Appealing to (
5.19b
) and (
5.19e
), we have that (
A.5
) is satisfied. From (
2.19
) at level
q
, which follows from Proposition
5.6
, the choice of
\nu
from earlier, and (
8.30
), we have that
Dv=Dv_{\ell_{q}}
satisfies the bound (
A.6
).

Proof of items (
1
) and (
2
) for
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
and
n={n_{\rm max}}+1
.

We first assume that
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
. With the goal of verifying (
i
)–(

) of Proposition
A.2
, we choose
\zeta,\mu,\Lambda,\rho
and
\varrho
as

\displaystyle\zeta=\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}\,,\qquad\mu

\displaystyle=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\,,\qquad\Lambda=\lambda_{q+1}\,,

(7.33)

\displaystyle\varrho=\mathbb{P}_{\left[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1\right]}\left(\left(\varrho_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\vartheta=\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}^{2{\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathbb{P}_{\left[q,{n_{\rm max}}+1\right]}\left(\varrho^{2}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)\,,

where we recall that
\varrho_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
is defined in Propositions
3.2
and
3.3
. We then have by definition that (
i
) from Proposition
A.2
is satisfied. By property (
1
) of Proposition
3.2
, we have that the functions
\varrho
and
\vartheta
defined in (
7.33
) are both periodic to scale
\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{-1}
, and so (
ii
) is satisfied. In the case
p=1
, the estimates in (
A.7
) follow with
\mathcal{C}_{*,1}=1
from standard Littlewood-Paley arguments (see also the discussion in part (b) of
[
5
, Remark A.21]
) and item (
5
) from Proposition
3.3
. In the case
p=\infty
, the estimates follow from Lemma
3.5
, (
3.11b
) with the choices
\mathcal{C}_{*,\infty}=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}
,
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}},\lambda_{2}=\infty,\lambda=\lambda_{q+1},r=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
. We recall from (
8.36
) the choice of
\alpha=\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\frac{b-1}{b}
, so that the loss
\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}
gives exactly a loss of
\Gamma_{q+1}
. From (
8.20
), (
8.24
), and the temporary assumption that
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
, we have that

\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\ll\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\ll\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\leq\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}\leq\lambda_{q+1},

and so (
A.8
) is satisfied. From (
8.37
) we have that

\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\leq\left(\frac{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}{2\pi\sqrt{3}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}=\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q+1}}{2\pi\sqrt{3}}\right)^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}\,,

and so (
A.9
) is satisfied. Applying the estimate (
A.11
) for
p=1
with
\alpha
as in (
8.36
), recalling the value for
\mathcal{C}_{G,1}
in (
7.30
), summing over
i
and using (
2.11
) at level
q
, summing over
j,k,\xi
, summing over
\vec{l}
and using (
5.43
) with
r_{1}=\infty
and
r_{2}=2
, and appealing to (
8.38
) and (
8.40
), we obtain that for
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{8}\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}^{-1}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.34)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

Applying the same steps but in the case
p=\infty
and using the parameter inequality (
8.45
) yields the bound

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+9}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

in the same range of
N,M
. The proof is complete after using (
8.58c
), which gives that the range of derivatives allowed in (
7.34
) and (
7.35
) is as much as is needed in (
7.27a
).

Following the parameter choices in
[
5
, Remark A.19]
, we set
N_{\circ}=M_{\circ}=3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, and
N^{\sharp}=\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5
. From (
8.58d
), we have that the condition
N_{\circ}\leq\nicefrac{{N^{\sharp}}}{{4}}
is satisfied.
The inequalities (
A.13
) and (
A.14
) follow from the discussion in
[
5
, Remark A.19]
. The inequality in (
A.15
) follows from the choices
\lambda=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
,
\zeta=\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}\geq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
, (
8.32
), and (
8.50
). Having satisfied these assumptions, we may now appeal to estimate (
A.17
) for
p=\infty
and sum over all parameters
(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l})
. Since
\vec{l}
takes at most
\lambda_{q+1}^{3}
values,
i
, and

are bounded independently of
q
, and
k
corresponds to a partition of unity in time, we obtain (
7.26
) for the case
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
and
n={n_{\rm max}}+1
.

Recall that we began this case with the temporary assumption that
{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
. In the case
{\widetilde{n}}={n_{\rm max}}
, we have from (
8.24
) that
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{n_{\rm max}}}>\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}
. Then we can set
\zeta=\mu=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{n_{\rm max}}}
and substitute
\mathbb{P}_{\geq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
for
\mathbb{P}_{[q,{n_{\rm max}}]}
. The only change is that (
7.34
) and (
7.35
) become stronger, since
\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}<\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{n_{\rm max}}}
, and so the desired estimates follow by arguing as before. We omit further details.

Proof of item (
3
) and of item (
1
) when
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
and
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
.

We set

(7.36)

\displaystyle\zeta

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\max\left\{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}},\lambda_{q,n-1}\right\}&\mbox{if}\quad 2\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}\\
\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}&\mbox{if}\quad n=1\,,\end{cases}\qquad\mu=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\,,\qquad\Lambda=\lambda_{q,n}\,,

and

\displaystyle\varrho

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\left(\varrho_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{2}\right)\,,\qquad\vartheta=\zeta^{2{\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\mathbb{P}_{[q,n]}\left(\varrho^{2}_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)\,.

We then have by definition that (
i
) from Proposition
A.2
is satisfied. By property (
1
) of Proposition
3.2
,
\varrho
and
\vartheta
are both periodic to scale
\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{-1}
, and so (

) is satisfied. The estimates in (
A.7
) follow with
\mathcal{C}_{*,1}=1
in the case

as before. In the case

, we appeal to Lemma
3.5
, (
3.11b
) with

,

, and

to deduce that (

) holds with

. We again set
\alpha
as in (
8.36
). From (
8.24
) and the condition that
r({\widetilde{n}})<n
, we have that if

, then

\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\ll\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\leq\max\left\{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}},\lambda_{q,n-1}\right\}\leq\lambda_{q,n}\,,

and so (
A.8
) is satisfied if

. If

, then it must be the case that
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, and so

\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,0}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\ll\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,0}\leq\lambda_{q,1}\,.

From (
8.37
), the inequality
\lambda_{q,n}\leq\lambda_{q+1}
, and the choices of
\mu
and
\lambda
, we have that (
A.9
) is satisfied.

We now use the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{G,p}
in (
7.30
) and apply the estimate (
A.11
). In the case that
p=1
and
n=1
, then we must have
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, and so for all
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}
, we sum over
(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l})
as before and obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{0,1}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\delta_{q+1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\bigl(\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}\bigr)^{-1}\lambda_{q,1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{0}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.37)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,1}\lambda_{q,1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{0}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The inequality in the last line follows immediately from the definitions in (
8.26
). Alternatively, if
n>1
, then
\zeta^{-1}\leq\lambda_{q,n-1}^{-1}
from (
7.36
), and so if

,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},n}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{8}\lambda_{q,n-1}^{-1}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+1,n}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

In the last inequality, we have used (
8.42
). After using (
8.59
), which gives
\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}\geq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
for all
{\widetilde{n}}<n
, we have achieved (
7.28a
).

In the case that
p=\infty
and
n=1
, then we must have that
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, and so

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{0,1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\lambda_{q,0}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q,1}}{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,0}}\right)^{2}\left(\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{2}\right)^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\lambda_{q,1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{0}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.39)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\lambda_{q,1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{0}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

To achieve the last line, we have appealed to (
8.24
) and the inequality
\frac{\lambda_{q,1}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}}<1
, which is immediate from a large choice of
{n_{\rm max}}
. In the case that
p=\infty
and
n\geq 2
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{n,{\widetilde{n}}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+9}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{q,n}}{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{2}\lambda_{q,n-1}^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+13}\frac{\lambda_{q,n}^{2}}{\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n-1}}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon(n)}\lambda_{q,n}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

To achieve the second inequality, we have used (
8.24
). To achieve the third inequality, we have used (
8.39
) and (
6.7
). The estimates above are again valid in the range
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}
, which from (
8.59
) completes the proof of (
7.28b
).

Following again the parameter choices in
[
5
, Remark A.19]
, we set
N_{\circ}=M_{\circ}=3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, and
N^{\sharp}=\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5
. From (
8.58d
), we have that the condition
N_{\circ}\leq\nicefrac{{N^{\sharp}}}{{4}}
is satisfied. The inequalities (
A.13
) and (
A.14
) follow from the discussion in
[
5
, Remark A.19]
. The inequality in (
A.15
) follows from the choices
\lambda=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
,
\zeta\geq\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
, (
8.32
), and (
8.50
). We then achieve the concluded estimate in (
A.17
), which after summing as before gives (
7.26
) in the remaining cases
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}<{n_{\rm max}}
,
r({\widetilde{n}})<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
.
∎

7.5.
Type 2 oscillation errors

In order to show that the Type 2 errors identified in (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) vanish, we will apply Proposition
3.8
on the support of a specific cutoff function

\eta=\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}=\psi_{i,q}\chi_{i,k,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,

in order to place pipes parallel to
\xi
on
\mathrm{supp\,}\eta
. We first collect several preliminary estimates in the first subsubsection, mainly with the goal of verifying assumption (
3
) from Proposition
3.8
, before applying Proposition
3.8
in the second.

7.5.1.
Preliminary estimates

Lemma 7.6

(
Keeping Track of Overlap).

For every tuple
(i,j,k,n)
, define the index set
\mathcal{I}
as

\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}(i,j,k,n)=\bigl\{({i^{*}},{j^{*}},q,{n^{*}})\colon{n^{*}}\leq n,\;\psi_{i,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n}\chi_{i,k,q}\psi_{{i^{*}},q}\omega_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},q,{n^{*}}}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\not\equiv 0\bigr\}\,.

Then, the cardinality of
\mathcal{I}
is bounded above by
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}\Gamma_{q+1}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{\eta}
depends only on
{n_{\rm max}}
,
{j_{\rm max}}
, and dimensional constants. In particular,
C_{\eta}
is independent of
q
.

Proof of Lemma
7.6
.

The proof proceeds similarly to the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 8.8]
. In fact it is somewhat simpler, since the parameter
p
(see
[
5
, Definition 2.4]
) is no longer part of the scheme, and we are not considering the checkerboard cutoffs
\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
yet, but will only incorporate them later. We thus give only an idea of the proof. Once
i
is fixed, we first note that
\psi_{i,q}
may only overlap with
\psi_{i+1,q}
and
\psi_{i-1,q}
from (
2.11
) at level
q
. The factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}
in the upper bound for the cardinality of
\mathcal{I}
comes from the fact that the timescale of the
\chi_{i+1,{k^{*}},q}
’s on the support of
\psi_{i+1,q}
is faster by a factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}
than the timescale of the
\chi_{i,k,q}
’s on the support of
\psi_{i,q}
. Considering then values of
j
and
n
introduces a dependence on
{j_{\rm max}}
and
{n_{\rm max}}
which is nevertheless independent of
q
.
∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
(x,t),(y,t)\in\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}
be such that
\psi_{i,q}^{2}(x,t)\geq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
and
\psi_{i,q}^{2}(y,t)\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}
. Then there exists a geometric constant
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}>1
such that

(7.41)

|x-y|\geq\mathcal{C}_{*}\left(\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q}\right)^{-1}.

For the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we refer to
[
5
, Lemma 8.9]
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Consider cutoff functions

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle:=\eta_{i,j,k,q,n,\xi,\vec{l}}=\psi_{i,q}\chi_{i,k,q}\omega_{i,j,q,n}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}},

\displaystyle\eta^{*}

\displaystyle:=\eta_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},q,{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}=\psi_{{i^{*}},q}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\omega_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},q,{n^{*}}}\zeta_{q,{i^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}},

where
({i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}})\in\mathcal{I}(i,j,k,n)
, as defined in Lemma
7.6
. Let
t^{*}\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
be given. Assume furthermore that
\eta\eta^{*}\not\equiv 0
, which implies that
\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\,\zeta_{q,{i^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\not\equiv 0
. Then there exists a convex set
\Omega:=\Omega(\eta,\eta^{*},t^{*})\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
with diameter
\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}
such that

\bigl(\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}\bigr)\subset\Omega\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i\pm,q}\,.

Proof of Lemma
7.8
.

Let
(x,t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\eta\eta^{*}\right)
. Then there exists
i^{\prime}\in\{i-1,i,i+1\}
such that
\psi_{i^{\prime},q}^{2}(x,t_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}
. Consider the flow
X(x,t)
originating from
(x,t_{0})
. Then for any
t
such that
|t-t_{0}|\leq\tau_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-i+5+\mathsf{c_{0}}}
, we can apply Lemma
5.8
to deduce that
\psi_{i^{\prime},q}^{2}(t,X(x,t))\geq\frac{1}{4}
. By the definition of
\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
, the fact that
{i^{*}}\in\{i-1,i,i+1\}
, the existence of
(x,t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}(\chi_{i,k,q}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q})
, and the fact that
t^{*}\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
, we in particular deduce that
\psi_{i^{\prime},q}^{2}(t^{*},X(x,t^{*}))\geq\frac{1}{4}
. Now, let
y
be such that

|X(x,t^{*})-y|\leq\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{-1}<\mathcal{C}_{*}(\Gamma_{q}\lambda_{q})^{-1}

for
\mathcal{C}_{*}
given in (
7.41
), where we have used the definition of
\lambda_{q,n}
in (
8.23
). Then from Lemma
7.7
, it cannot be the case that
\psi_{i^{\prime},q}^{2}(y,t^{*})\leq\frac{1}{8}
, and so

(7.42)

\displaystyle y\in\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i^{\prime},q}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i\pm,q}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}\,.

Since
y
is arbitrary, we conclude that the ball of radius
\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda_{q,n}^{-1}
is contained in
\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i\pm,q}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}
. We let
\Omega(\eta,\eta^{*},t^{*})
to be precisely this ball. Since
D_{t,q}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}=0
and
(x,t_{0})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
, we have that
X(x,t^{*})\in\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}
. Then, recalling that the support of
\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}
must obey the diameter bound in (
5.41
) on the support of
\widetilde{\chi}_{i,k,q}
, which contains the support of
\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
by (
5.18
), we conclude that

(7.43)

\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{q,i,k,n,\xi,\vec{l}}\cap\{t=t^{*}\}\subset\Omega\,.

Combining (
7.42
) and (
7.43
) concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

As in Lemma
7.8
, consider cutoff functions
\eta
and
\eta^{*}
satisfying the conditions from Lemma
7.6
and the assumption
\eta\eta^{*}\not\equiv 0
. Let
t^{*}\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
be such that
\Phi^{*}:=\Phi_{({i^{*}},{k^{*}})}
is the identity at time
t^{*}
. Using Lemma
7.8
, define
\Omega:=\Omega(\eta,\eta^{*},t^{*})
. Define
\Omega(t):=\Omega(\eta,\eta^{*},t^{*},t):=X^{*}(\Omega,t)
, where
X^{*}
is the inverse of
\Phi^{*}
. Then the following conclusions hold.

(1)

For
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
,

\mathrm{supp\,}\eta(\cdot,t)\subset\Omega(t)\subset\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i\pm,q}\,.

(2)

Let
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}:=\mathbb{W}_{\xi*,q+1,n^{*}}^{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\circ\Phi_{({i^{*}},{k^{*}})}
be the intermittent pipe flow supported on
\eta^{*}
. Then
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}
satisfies the conclusion of Lemma
3.7
on the set
\Omega(t)
for
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
.

(3)

For
\mathcal{I}=\mathcal{I}(i,j,k,n)
defined as in Lemma
7.6
, we denote

(7.44)

\mathsf{P}:=\bigcup_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\psi_{{i^{*}},q}\omega_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},q,{n^{*}}}\right)\bigcap\left(\bigcup_{\vec{l}^{*},{\xi^{*}}}\mathrm{supp\,}\left(\zeta_{q,{i^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\mathbb{W}_{{\xi^{*}},q+1,{n^{*}}}^{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},\vec{l}}\circ\Phi_{({i^{*}},{k^{*}})}\right)\right)\right)\,,

which is precisely the union of the supports of all pipes living on cutoff functions indexed by tuples belonging to
\mathcal{I}
, which are however
not
restricted to the support of their corresponding time cutoffs
\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
. Then there exists
\mathcal{C}_{P}
such that for any convex set
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{T}^{3}
with
\textnormal{diam}(\Omega^{\prime})\leq(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
and any
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
, the set
\mathsf{P}\cap\left(\{t\}\times\Omega^{\prime}\right)
consists of at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}
segments of deformed pipes of length
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
.

Remark 7.10
.

The third item simply asserts that at stage
n
, there exists a geometric constant
\mathcal{C}_{P}
such that in any
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}}})^{3}
-periodic cell of diameter approximately
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
, there exist at most
\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}
segments of deformed pipes of length
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
. This will later allow us to apply Proposition
3.8
. The factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}
comes from the fact that overlapping time cutoffs
\chi_{i,k,q}
and
\chi_{i+1,k^{\prime},q}
have timescales which differ by a factor of
\Gamma_{q+1}
, and that we have
not
restricted
\mathbb{W}_{{\xi^{*}},q+1,{n^{*}}}^{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}
to the support of its corresponding time cutoff
\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}
. Notice also that since choosing a shift moves a segment of pipe inside a
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}})^{3}
-periodic cell but does not increase the
number
of such segments, the conclusion in (
3
) is independent of the choice of placement. We may thus appeal to it in the next subsection in order to choose a placement.

Proof of Lemma
7.9
.

The statement and proof are quite similar to the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 8.11]
, and we refer there for the proof of the first two claims. The only difference is contained in the third claim above, since we have rephrased the way in which we count the number of deformed segments of pipe comprising
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}
which may overlap with
\mathrm{supp\,}\eta
. We remind the reader that a single “segment of deformed pipe” consists of the support of
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}
restricted to a single (deformed)
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}^{3}}}{{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}}})^{-1}
-periodic cell. Then to prove the third claim, we first fix a tuple
({i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}})\in\mathcal{I}
and note that in any convex set
\Omega^{\prime}
of diameter at most
(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n})^{-1}
, the conclusions of Lemma
3.7
and the construction of the checkerboard cutoff functions implies that there exist at most finitely many
\zeta_{q,{i^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}
such that

\psi_{{i^{*}},q}\omega_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},q,{n^{*}}}\chi_{{i^{*}},{k^{*}},q}\zeta_{q,{i^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\not\equiv 0\,.

From the construction of
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}
in Proposition
3.3
and the fact that
\mathbb{W}^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}
satisfies the conclusions of Lemma
3.7
on
\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
, we then have that taking the union over just
\vec{l}^{*}
and
{\xi^{*}}
in (
7.44
) allows for the desired conclusion with a
q
-independent constant. Then applying Lemma (
7.7
) and taking the union over the
C_{\eta}\Gamma_{q+1}
many tuples in
\mathcal{I}
then provides the conclusion with a new constant
\mathcal{C}_{P}
multiplied by
\Gamma_{q+1}
.
∎

7.5.2.
Applying Proposition
3.8

Lemma 7.11
.

The Type 2 oscillation errors identified in (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) vanish.

Proof of Lemma
7.11
.

To show that the errors defined in (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) vanish, it suffices to show the following: for any pairs of cutoff functions
\eta=\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}
and
\eta^{*}=\eta_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},q,{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}
where
({i^{*}},{j^{*}},{n^{*}},{k^{*}})\in\mathcal{I}(i,j,k,n)
, we have that

(7.45)

\displaystyle\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}\;\eta_{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},q,{n^{*}},{\xi^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\;\Bigl(\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\otimes\mathbb{W}_{{\xi^{*}},q+1,{n^{*}}}^{{i^{*}},{j^{*}},{k^{*}},{n^{*}},\vec{l}^{*}}\circ\Phi_{({i^{*}},{k^{*}})}\Bigr)\equiv 0\,.

The proof of this claim will proceed by fixing
{\widetilde{n}}
, using the preliminary estimates, and applying Proposition
3.8
.

Now, consider all cutoff functions
\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}
utilized at stage
{\widetilde{n}}
. We may choose an ordering of the tuples
(i,j,k,\xi,\vec{l})
at level
{\widetilde{n}}
, which automatically provides orderings for the cutoff functions
\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}
and associated pipe flows
\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
. To lighten the notation, we will abbreviate the newly ordered cutoff functions as
\eta_{z}
and the associated intermittent pipe flows as
(\mathbb{W}\circ\Phi)_{z}
, where
z\in\mathbb{N}
corresponds to the ordering. We will apply Proposition
3.8
inductively on
z\in\mathbb{N}
, according to the chosen ordering, so that (
7.45
) holds.

Fix
\eta_{z}
, and fix the associated index set
\mathcal{I}(z)=\mathcal{I}(i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}})
. Since we are proving (
7.45
) iteratively, we only need to consider the elements
z^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(z)
such that
{n^{*}}<{\widetilde{n}}
, and
\widehat{z}\in\mathcal{I}(z)
such that
{n^{*}}={\widetilde{n}}
and
\widehat{z}<z
, according to the aforementioned ordering.

We will apply Proposition
3.8
with the following choices. First, we recall that at the time
t_{z}
at which
\Phi_{z}
is the identity, the cutoff function
\eta_{z}
contains a checkerboard cutoff function
\zeta_{z}
which from (
5.35
) is adapted to a rectangular prism of dimensions
2\pi\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}
in the direction of
\xi_{z}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\Gamma_{q+1}(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})^{-1}
in the directions perpendicular to
\xi_{z}
. Thus we can bound the dimensions of the support of the anistropic checkerboard cutoff by
4\pi\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}
and
2\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}\Gamma_{q+1}(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})^{-1}
, and we thus set

\Omega=\mathrm{supp\,}\zeta_{z}\cap\{t=t_{z}\}\,,\qquad r_{1}=\frac{\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}}{4\pi\lambda_{q+1}}\,,\qquad r_{2}=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\,,\qquad\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}=2\mathcal{C}_{\Gamma}.

Recalling item
3
from Lemma
7.9
, we choose the support of
(\mathbb{W}\circ\Phi)_{z}|_{t=t_{z}}
to have empty intersection with

(7.46)

\mathsf{P}\cap\Omega\,,\qquad\mathsf{P}\textnormal{ as defined in }\eqref{eq:counting:pipelets}\,,

and so by definition
\mathsf{P}
satisfies item
3
from Proposition
3.8
. Thus it remains to check (
3.21
). From the definition of
r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
in (
8.24
), we have that

(7.47)

\displaystyle C_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}r_{2}^{2}=C_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\lesssim C_{*}\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}\mathcal{C}_{P}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3}\frac{\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-4}<r_{1}

if
a
is chosen sufficiently large so that
\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
can absorb the constants
C_{*}
,
\mathcal{C}_{\Omega}^{2}
,
\mathcal{C}_{P}
and the implicit constant, all of which are bounded independently of
q
. Therefore (
3.21
) is satisfied, and we may apply Proposition
3.8
to choose a placement for
\mathbb{W}_{z}
which has empty intersection with
\mathsf{P}
at time
t=t_{z}
. This shows that at time
t=t_{z}
,
\mathbb{W}_{z}
has empty intersection with
all
previously existing pipes which may be non-zero at
any
time
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
but have been flowed to time
t=t_{z}
. Finally, since
D_{t,q}(\mathbb{W}\circ\Phi)_{z}=D_{t,q}(\mathbb{W}\circ\Phi)_{z^{\prime}}=D_{t,q}(\mathbb{W}\circ\Phi)_{\widehat{z}}
, and
\mathsf{P}
has been constructed to contain
all
pipes which are non-zero at
any
time
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{i,k,q}
, (
7.45
) is satisfied for all
t\in\mathrm{supp\,}\chi_{z}
, concluding the proof.
∎

7.6.
Divergence corrector errors

In this subsection we define and estimate the stress
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}
written in (
7.24
) and arising from the divergence correctors identified in (
7.17
), which satisfy

\mathrm{div\,}\bigl(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\bigr)=\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}\right)\,.

Lemma 7.12
.

For all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the divergence corrector errors
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}
satisfy the bounds

(7.48a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(7.48b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}\tau_{q}^{-1},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

for all
k,m\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
.

Proof of Lemma
7.12
.

We first present the estimates for the stress
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}=w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}
, which is also given explicitly by the last line in (
7.17
) and may be absorbed directly into
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}
and estimated. By the Leibniz rule, the estimate (
7.12a
) with
(r,r_{1},r_{2})=(2,\infty,1)
, and the fact that
\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\cap\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime},k^{\prime})}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
|i^{\prime}-i|\leq 1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}\right\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{6}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The bound (
7.48a
) for
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}
now follows from the parameter inequality (
8.52
).
Similarly, from (
7.12b
) it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+7}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The bound (
7.48b
) for
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}
then follows from the inequality (
8.54
).

It thus remains to analyze
\mathrm{div\,}(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)})
. Using the second line of (
7.17
), we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{div\,}\bigl(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}+w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(c)}\otimes w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{(p)}\bigr)^{\bullet}

(7.49)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{m}\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\varrho_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\xi^{\ell}\bigl(A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}+A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}\bigr)\partial_{p}a_{(\xi)}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\bigr)

where
\epsilon_{i_{1}i_{2}i_{3}}
is the Levi-Civita alternating tensor, we implicitly contract the repeated indices
\ell,m,p,r,s
, and the
\bullet
refers to the indices of the vectors on either side of the above display. The subtle point is that if the derivative in
\partial_{p}a_{(\xi)}
is not in a good direction, cf. Lemma
5.18
, one seemingly obtains the wrong bound. As such we use that
\{\xi,\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime}\}
is an orthonormal basis associated with the direction vector
\xi
with
\xi\times\xi^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime\prime}
, and so
\xi^{n}\xi^{\ell}+(\xi^{\prime})^{n}(\xi^{\prime})^{\ell}+(\xi^{\prime\prime})^{n}(\xi^{\prime\prime})^{\ell}=\delta^{n\ell}
, and decompose
\partial_{p}a_{(\xi)}
into a sum of vector fields
a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good}
and
a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}
defined by

(7.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{p}a_{(\xi)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{n}\xi^{n}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}a_{(\xi)}}_{=:a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good}}+\underbrace{\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{n}(\xi^{\prime})^{n}(\xi^{\prime})^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}a_{(\xi)}+\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{n}(\xi^{\prime\prime})^{n}(\xi^{\prime\prime})^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{j}\partial_{j}a_{(\xi)}}_{=:a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}}\,,

where we have also set
A=A_{(i,k)}=(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)})^{-1}
. Using this decomposition, we note that from Lemma
7.1
, the derivative of
a_{(\xi)}
in the “good” term costs a factor of
\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
, whereas the derivatives landing on
a_{(\xi)}
in the “bad” terms cost a factor of
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\gg\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
.

In view of (
7.8
) and (
7.9
), we leave the part of (
7.49
) which contains
a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good}
in divergence form and simply move the resulting symmetric stress

(7.51)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}^{\rm good})^{m\bullet}:=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}\varrho_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\xi^{\ell}\bigl(A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}+A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}\bigr)a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,,

into
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},\rm corr}
(and thus
\mathring{R}_{q+1}^{{\widetilde{n}}}
), up to removing a trace term which is thrown into the pressure. This good part of
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}
obeys the same
L^{1}
and
L^{\infty}
bounds as
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,3}
above. To see this, we apply the
L^{1}
de-correlation estimate from Lemma
A.1
, for
p=1
,
f=a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}(A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}+A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr})a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}
,
\Phi=\Phi_{(i,k)}
,
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
, and
\varphi=\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}
. In light of Proposition
3.3
, Corollary
5.10
, estimate (
7.8
), and definition (
7.50
), we have that the assumptions of Lemma
A.1
hold with the parameter choices
\mathcal{C}_{f}=|\mathrm{supp\,}\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}}|\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{j+7}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\lambda=\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}
,
\nu=\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}\tau_{q}^{-1}
,
\widetilde{\nu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
,
\mu=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}=\Gamma_{q+1}\lambda
,
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
,
\zeta=\widetilde{\zeta}=\lambda_{q+1}
,
N_{x}=0
, and
N_{\circ}=\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5
. By (
8.58a
) we have that
N_{\circ}\geq 2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4
, and by (
8.37
) we have that
\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\leq(\Gamma_{q+1}(2\pi\sqrt{3})^{-1})^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}
, and so condition (
A.2
) is verified. Thus, from (
A.3
) and summing on
\vec{l}
using (
5.43
), we deduce the
L^{1}
estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}^{\rm good}\bigr\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The bound (
7.48a
) for
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}^{\rm good}
now follows from the parameter inequality (
8.53
), and the fact that
\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\geq\max\{2\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}+4+3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}},6\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}\}
, which is a consequence of (
8.58a
) and (
8.58c
). Similarly, from Proposition
3.3
, Corollary
5.10
, estimate (
7.9
), and definition (
7.50
), we have the
L^{\infty}
estimate

\displaystyle\bigl\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}^{\rm good}\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}\lesssim r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+8}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

The bound (
7.48b
) for
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{n}},1,2}^{\rm good}
then follows from the parameter inequality
(
8.47
).

Returning to (
7.49
), it remains to consider the bad part, coming from the second term in (
7.50
), namely

(7.52)

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{m}\left(a_{(\xi)}\varrho_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\xi^{\ell}\bigl(A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}+A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}\bigr)a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)=\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\bullet}+\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\bullet}

where
\mathbf{V}_{1}
corresponds to the term containing
A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}
, and
\mathbf{V}_{2}
corresponds to the term containing
A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}
. When we distribute the
\partial_{m}
derivative in (
7.52
), we need to be careful that the derivative does not land on the fast (at frequency
\lambda_{q+1}
) object
\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}
.

Let us first handle
\mathbf{V}_{1}
. For this purpose, note that

\displaystyle\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\partial_{m}\left((\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

\displaystyle=\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\partial_{m}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{r}\left(\partial_{r}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=\left(\xi^{\ell}\partial_{\ell}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle=0

because
\xi\cdot\nabla
annihilates both
\varrho_{(\xi)}
and
\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
, from (
3.4
).
Thus, by (
7.52
), the term
\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\bullet}
becomes

(7.53)

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{1}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{m}\bigl(a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\bigr)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

Notice that by the Piola identity, we have
\partial_{m}(a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s})=\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\partial_{m}(a_{(\xi)}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s})
, and so the slow objects contain a derivative that costs the good factor of
\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
, and a derivative that costs the bad factor of
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
.
We then apply the inverse divergence operator
\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}
from Proposition
A.2
, with the following choices:
p=1
,
G=\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{m}\partial_{m}(a_{(\xi)}\epsilon_{\bullet pr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s})
,
\varrho=\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}
,
\Phi=\Phi_{(i,k)}
,
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
, and
N_{*}=M_{*}=\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5)\rfloor
. By (
2.19
), Corollary
5.10
, and estimate (
7.8
), assumption (
A.4
) holds for
\mathcal{C}_{G}=|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{j+7}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\lambda=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
,
N_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
,
\nu=\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}
, and
\widetilde{\nu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
, while assumptions (
A.5
)–(
A.6
) hold with
\lambda^{\prime}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
. From Proposition
3.3
and standard Littlewood-Paley analysis, upon letting
\zeta=\mu=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\vartheta=(\zeta^{-2}\Delta)^{-{\mathsf{d}}}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})
(we note that
\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}
has mean zero from a direct computation using the definition of the intermittent pipe flows from Proposition
3.3
),
\Lambda=\lambda_{q+1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{*}=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
, and
\alpha
as in (
8.36
), we have that condition (
A.7
) is satisfied.
With these chosen parameters, the condition (
A.8
) trivially holds, while condition (
A.9
) is equivalent to
\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\leq(\Gamma_{q+1}(2\pi\sqrt{3})^{-1})^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}
, which in this case holds due to (
8.37
). Conditions (
A.13
)–(
A.14
) are verified for
N_{\circ}=M_{\circ}=3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{v}=\Gamma_{q+1}^{{i_{\rm max}}+1}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{2}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{2}
, in view of (
2.1
), (
2.13
), and (
2.20
), and (
8.20
)–(
8.21
). Lastly, the inequality (
A.15
) holds because
{\mathsf{d}}
is taken to be sufficiently large to ensure (
8.51
). From (
A.11
), (
A.17
), and a sum on
\vec{l}
as before, we deduce the
L^{1}
bound

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\mathbf{V}_{1}\right\|_{L^{1}}

(7.54)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{8}\frac{(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}}{(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,.

Since
\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}
– see (
8.53
), and
\lfloor\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}\geq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
– see (
8.58c
), the above bound is consistent with (
7.48a
).

The
L^{\infty}
estimate is obtained similarly. We again apply Proposition
A.2
with the only parameters that change being:
p=\infty
,
\mathcal{C}_{G}=\Gamma_{q}^{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+8}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
) – see (
A.14
), and
\mathcal{C}_{*}=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}
– see (
3.5
) and (
3.6
).
From (
A.11
) and (
A.17
) we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*})\mathbf{V}_{1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

(7.55)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+9}\frac{(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}}{(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\lambda_{q+1}}\lambda_{q+1}^{k}\mathcal{M}\left(m,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)\,,

Since
\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+8}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}
, see (
8.47
), the above bound is consistent with (
7.48b
).
