It remains to consider the term
\mathbf{V}_{2}
in (
7.52
). We distribute the
\partial_{m}
derivative on either the slow or the fast objects and decompose

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{2}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\partial_{m}\left(a_{(\xi)}\varrho_{(\xi)}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}\Bigl(\partial_{m}\bigl(\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\bigr)a_{(\xi)}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}+a_{m,(\xi)}^{\rm good}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \ -a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{m}(a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good})\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\Bigr)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s}\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

(7.56)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi,i,j,k,\vec{l}}a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\partial_{m}\left((\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\,.

In the second equality above we have used the identities
\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{m}(a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad})=-\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{m}(a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm good})
, and that
\epsilon_{mpr}a_{m,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}=0
.
We first consider the terms in which the
\partial_{m}
has not landed on functions related to pipe densities. Similarly to the definition of
\mathbf{V}_{1}
in (
7.53
), the slow functions in each term contain a derivative that costs the good factor of
\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}
, and a derivative that costs the bad factor of
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
. As such, when applying
\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}
to the second to last line of (
7.56
), the resulting stress obeys exactly the same estimates as (
7.54
) and (
7.55
).

Finally, we are left to consider the term on the last line of (
7.56
), in which the
\partial_{m}
derivative lands on the fast objects, at frequency
\lambda_{q+1}
. The key observation is that this term is in fact equal to
0
! To see this cancellation, we recall the identification of
a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}
in (
7.50
), and we recall from (
3.3
) that
\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}=-\xi^{\prime}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime\prime}+\xi^{\prime\prime}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime}
.
With these identities, we have

\displaystyle a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\partial_{m}\left((\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=a_{(\xi)}\xi^{\ell}A_{\ell}^{\bullet}\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\partial_{n}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,.

Note that from (
7.50
), that
a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}
contains either a factor of
\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{k}\xi^{\prime}_{k}
or a factor of
\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{k}\xi^{\prime\prime}_{k}
. From (
3.4
), we also have that

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\partial_{n}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}^{s}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})

\displaystyle=-\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{\prime}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi^{\prime}_{n}\left((\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{\prime\prime}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi^{\prime}_{n}\left((\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime}\right)\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{\prime}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi_{n}^{\prime\prime}\left((\xi^{\prime\prime}\cdot\nabla)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{\prime\prime}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi_{n}^{\prime\prime}\left((\xi^{\prime}\cdot\nabla)\left(\varrho_{(\xi)}\varphi_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}^{\prime\prime}\right)\right)\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\,.

Thus, the expression
\epsilon_{mpr}a_{p,(\xi)}^{\rm bad}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\partial_{n}(\varrho_{(\xi)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{s})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
equals the sum of eight terms, each of which is of the type

\displaystyle\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{k}\xi^{(1)}_{k}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{(2)}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi^{(3)}_{n}\times(\mbox{product of fast pipe densities or fast cutoffs})\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}

where
(\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)},\xi^{(3)})\in\{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime}\}^{3}
. Since in each of these eight terms, at least two of the vectors in the tuple
(\xi^{(1)},\xi^{(2)},\xi^{(3)})
are equal to each other, either to
\xi^{\prime}
or
\xi^{\prime\prime}
, by the skew symmetry of the Levi-Civita symbol, we must have

\displaystyle\epsilon_{mpr}\partial_{p}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{k}\xi^{(1)}_{k}\partial_{r}\Phi_{(i,k)}^{s}\xi^{(2)}_{s}\partial_{m}\Phi^{n}\xi^{(3)}_{n}=0\,.

This proves that the last term on the right side of (
7.56
) is indeed equal to
0
, concluding the proof.
∎

7.7.
Transport errors

Lemma 7.13
.

For all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the transport error satisfies the following estimates for
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
:

(7.57a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(7.57b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\left(\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}})\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

Proof of Lemma
7.13
.

Recall from the first line of (
7.24
) that the transport error is given by
\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}
applied to
D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
, which we further expand as

\displaystyle D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}

\displaystyle=D_{t,q}\biggl(\sum_{\;i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}D_{t,q}\left(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\right)\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}+\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}\left(D_{t,q}\nabla a_{(\xi)}\right)\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

(7.58)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\left(\left(D_{t,q}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)

Since the second two terms contain the corrector defined in (
7.6
), and the bounds for the corrector in (
7.12a
) are stronger than that of the principal part of the perturbation, we shall completely estimate only the first term and simply indicate the set-up for the second and third. Before applying Proposition
A.2
, recall that the inverse divergence of (
7.58
) needs to be estimated on the support of a cutoff
\psi_{i,q}
in order to verify (
7.57a
) and (
7.57b
). Recall that for all
{\widetilde{n}}
,
D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
has zero mean. Thus, although each individual term in the final equality in (
7.58
) may not have zero mean, we can safely apply
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{R}^{*}
to each term and estimate the outputs while ignoring the last term in (
A.16
).

We will apply Proposition
A.2
to the first term with the following choices. Let
p\in\{1,\infty\}
. We set
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
, and
D_{t}=D_{t,q}=\partial_{t}+v_{\ell_{q}}\cdot\nabla
as usual. We set
N_{*}=M_{*}=\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor
, with
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
and
{\mathsf{d}}
satisfying (
8.58a
). We define

G=D_{t,q}(a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1})\xi\,,

with
\lambda=\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\nu=\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5}
,
M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
,
\widetilde{\nu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
. In order to obtain the value of the amplitude constant
\mathcal{C}_{G}
, which now depends on
p
, when
p=1
we use (
7.8
) with
r=1
and (
5.19g
), while when
p=\infty
we use (
7.9
) and (
5.19g
), obtaining

(7.59a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,1}

\displaystyle=|\mathrm{supp\,}(\eta_{i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l}})|\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{j+2}\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+3}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+\frac{7}{2}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{i_{\rm max}}-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}

(7.59b)

\displaystyle\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+9-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\delta_{q}^{-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+7\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}})+20+\mathsf{c}_{0}-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}\,.

In the above expressions we have used (
8.33
) to control
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
, (
5.9
) to control
\Gamma_{q+1}^{{i_{\rm max}}}
, and the definition of
\tau_{q}
from (
8.22
). We have that

\displaystyle\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}G\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}-1,\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.60)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\left(\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right),

for all
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor
after using (
8.35
),
and so (
A.4
) is satisfied. We set
\Phi=\Phi_{i,k}
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
. Appealing as usual to Corollary
5.10
and (
2.19
) with
q^{\prime}=q
, which is valid from Proposition
5.6
, we have that (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) are satisfied.

Referring to (
1
) from Proposition
3.3
, we set
\varrho=\varrho_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
and
\vartheta=\vartheta_{\xi,\lambda_{q+1},r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}
. Setting
\zeta=\lambda_{q+1}
, we have that (
i
) is satisfied. Setting
\mu=\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
and referring to (
2
) from Proposition
3.3
, we have that (
ii
) is satisfied. Setting
\Lambda=\zeta=\lambda_{q+1}
,
C_{*,p}=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\frac{2}{p}-1}
,
\alpha
as in (
8.36
), and referring to (
3.5
) and (
3.6
) from Proposition
3.3
, we have that (
A.7
) is satisfied. (
A.8
) is immediate from the definitions. Referring to (
8.37
), we have that (
A.9
) is satisfied.

After summing on
(i,j,k,{\widetilde{n}},\xi,\vec{l})
, using (
2.11
) at level
q
, and (
5.43
) with
r_{1}=r_{2}=2
, we conclude from (
A.11
) that for
p=1
and
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\mathcal{H}\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+9-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.61)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

after also using (
8.55
) and (
8.33
). From (
8.58c
), these bounds are valid for all
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
. Similarly, for
p=\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\mathcal{H}\left(D_{t,q}w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+7\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}})+21+\mathsf{c}_{0}-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

(7.62)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+5},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right)

after also using (
8.27
) and (
8.56
).

To conclude the proof, we must still estimate the nonlocal (
\mathcal{R}^{*}
) portion of the inverse divergence, and the error terms coming from the divergence correctors. These error terms, however, obey stronger estimates than the bounds in (
7.61
) and (
7.62
), and so we refer to the proof of
[
5
, Lemma 8.4]
for further details.
∎

7.8.
Nash errors

Lemma 7.14
.

For all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the Nash errors satisfy the following estimates for
N,M\leq 3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
:

(7.63a)

\displaystyle\left\|\psi_{i,q}D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}\left(\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)\left(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{q+2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)

(7.63b)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}D_{t,q}^{m}\left(\left(\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}\right)(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}})\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}\psi_{i,q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4},\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)\,.

Proof of Lemma
7.14
.

Recall from the first line of (
7.24
) that the Nash error is given by
\mathcal{H}+\mathcal{R}^{*}
applied to
w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}
, which we further expand as

\displaystyle w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}\mathrm{curl\,}\left(a_{\xi,i,j,k,q,{\widetilde{n}},\vec{l}}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}

(7.64)

\displaystyle=\Biggl(\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}\nabla a_{(\xi)}\times\left(\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{T}\mathbb{U}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\right)+\sum_{i,j,k,\vec{l},\xi}a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i,k)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}\Biggr)\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}\,.

Due to the fact that the first term arises from the addition of the corrector defined in (
7.6
), and the fact that the bounds for the corrector in (
7.12a
) are stronger than that of the principal part of the perturbation, we shall only consider the second term. Note that the Nash error can be written as
\mathrm{div\,}(w_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\otimes v_{\ell_{q}})
and so has zero mean. Thus, although each individual term in the final equality in (
7.64
) may not have zero mean, we can safely apply
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{R}^{*}
to each term and estimate the outputs while ignoring the last term in (
A.16
).

We will apply Proposition
A.2
to the second term with the following choices. We set
v=v_{\ell_{q}}
, and
D_{t}=D_{t,q}=\partial_{t}+v_{\ell_{q}}\cdot\nabla
as usual. We set
N_{*}=M_{*}=\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-4\right)\rfloor
, with
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
and
{\mathsf{d}}
satisfying (
8.58a
). We define

G=a_{(\xi)}\nabla\Phi_{(i^{\prime},k)}^{-1}\xi\cdot\nabla v_{\ell_{q}}

and set
\mathcal{C}_{G,1}
,
\mathcal{C}_{G,\infty}
to be equal to the quantities in (
7.59
),
\lambda=\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}
,
\nu=\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i-\mathsf{c}_{\widetilde{\textnormal{n}}}+4}
,
M_{t}=\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, and
\widetilde{\nu}=\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}
. Note that these choices match exactly the choices from the estimates on the transport error. From (
7.8
) with
r=1
and
r_{1}=r_{2}=2
, (
5.19g
), and (
2.19
) at level
q
, we have that for
N,M\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-4\right)\rfloor

(7.65)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t,q}^{M}G\right\|_{L^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G,p}\left(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\right)^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},\tau_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{i+1},\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\right),

and so (
A.4
) is satisfied. Note that we have used (
8.30
) when converting the
\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
coming from (
2.19
) at level
q
to a
\tau_{q}^{-1}
. Setting
\Phi=\Phi_{(i,k)}
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}
, we have that (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) are satisfied as usual. The choices of
\varrho
,
\vartheta
,
\zeta
,
\mu
,
\Lambda
, and
\mathcal{C}_{*}
are identical to those of the transport error (both terms contain
\mathbb{W}_{\xi,q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\circ\Phi_{(i,k)}
), and so we have that (
i
)-(
ii
), (
A.7
), (
A.8
), and (
A.9
) are satisfied as well. Since the bound (
7.65
) is identical to that of (
7.60
), we obtain an estimate identical to (
7.61
) in the case
p=1
. The case
p=\infty
and the estimates for the
\mathcal{R}^{*}
portion follows analogously to that for the first term from the transport error. We omit further details.
∎

8.
Parameters

The purpose of the first subsection is to define the
q
-independent parameters
in order
, beginning with the regularity index
\beta
, and ending with the number
a_{*}
, which will be used to absorb every implicit constant throughout the paper. Then in Section
8.2
, we define the parameters which depend on
q
, as well as the parameters which depend in addition on
n
. Section
8.3
contains, in no particular order, consequences of the definitions made in the previous two sections which are necessary to close the estimates in the proof.

8.1.
Definitions and hierarchy of the parameters

The parameters in our construction are chosen as:

(i)

Choose an
L^{2}
regularity index
\beta\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})
; in light of
[
4
,
36
]
, there is no reason to take
\beta<\nicefrac{{1}}{{3}}
.

(ii)

Choose
b\in(1,\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})
sufficiently small such that

(8.1)

\displaystyle 2\beta b

\displaystyle<1\,.

(iii)

With
\beta
and
b
chosen, we may now designate a number of parameters:

(a)

The parameter
{n_{\rm max}}
, which denotes the total number of higher order stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
, is defined as the smallest integer such that

(8.2a)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{{n_{\rm max}}+1}

\displaystyle<\frac{(b-1)^{2}}{2b}

(8.2b)

\displaystyle 2\beta b+\frac{3+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}+\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}\,.

Notice that the second inequality is possible since
2\beta b<1
.

(b)

The parameter
{\mathsf{C}_{b}}
appearing in (
2.17
) to quantify
\left\|\psi_{i,q}\right\|_{L^{1}}
is defined as

(8.3)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{C}_{b}}=\frac{b+4}{b-1}\,.

(c)

The exponent
\mathsf{C_{R}}
is a small parameter used to estimate the Reynolds stress, cf. (
2.10a
), and then absorb geometric constants in the construction. It is defined as

(8.4)

\displaystyle\mathsf{C_{R}}=10b+1\,.

(iv)

The parameter
\mathsf{c_{0}}
, which is first introduced in (
2.16
) and utilized in Sections
6
and
7
to control small losses in the sharp material derivative estimates, is defined in terms of
{n_{\rm max}}
as

(8.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{c_{0}}=4{n_{\rm max}}+5\,.

(v)

The parameter
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}>0
, which is used in (
8.19
) to quantify the
finest
frequency scale between
\lambda_{q}
and
\lambda_{q+1}
utilized throughout the scheme, is defined as any real number such that

(8.6a)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\Gamma}300({n_{\rm max}}+1)(\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil)

\displaystyle<b-1

(8.6b)

\displaystyle 7\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil+22+4{n_{\rm max}})

\displaystyle<\frac{b-1}{2b}-\frac{3}{2(b-1)({n_{\rm max}}+1)}

(8.6c)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left(5+(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil)(9+{\mathsf{C}_{b}})\right)

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil}{{n_{\rm max}}}

(8.6d)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left(\mathsf{C_{R}}\left(\frac{b-1}{b}\right)+15+9(3+\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil)\right)

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\right)-2\beta b-\frac{3+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}\,

(8.6e)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+\mathsf{c}_{0}+10+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{C_{R}}\right)

\displaystyle<1-2\beta b

(8.6f)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left(7+\mathsf{C_{R}}+{n_{\rm max}}(8+{\mathsf{C}_{b}})\right)

\displaystyle<\frac{1-2\beta}{10}

(8.6g)

\displaystyle 2b\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(\mathsf{c}_{0}+7)<1-\beta\,.

We note that the right-hand side of (
8.6b
) is positive from (
8.2a
) and the right-hand sides of (
8.6c
) and (
8.6d
) are positive from (
8.2b
).

(vi)

The parameter
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
is defined as

(8.7)

{\mathsf{C_{u}}}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)({n_{\rm max}}+1)}\,.

(vii)

The parameter
\alpha>0
from the
L^{1}
loss of the inverse divergence operator is now defined as

(8.8)

\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}{2b}\,.

(viii)

The parameters
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
and
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}
are used in Section
5
in order to define the velocity and stress cutoff functions; see (
5.3
), (
5.7
), and (
5.24
). These large integers are chosen solely in terms of
b
and
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
as

(8.9)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}=\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}=\left\lceil\frac{3b}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}+\frac{15b}{2}\right\rceil\,.

(ix)

The parameter
\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, which is the number of sharp material derivatives propagated on stresses and velocities in Sections
2
through
7
, is chosen as the smallest integer for which we have

(8.10)

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}=\left\lceil\frac{4}{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)}\right\rceil\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}\,.

(x)

The parameter
\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}
, whose primary role is to quantify the number of sharp spatial derivatives propagated on the velocity increments and stresses, cf. (
2.8a
) and (
2.10a
), is chosen as the smallest integer for which we have the bound

(8.11)

\displaystyle 4b\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}+8+b(\mathsf{C_{R}}+3)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)+2\beta(b^{3}-1)

\displaystyle<\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}\,.

(xi)

The value of the decoupling parameter
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
, which is used in the
L^{p}
decorrellation conditions (
A.2
) and (
A.9
), is chosen as the smallest integer for which

(8.12)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}>\frac{8b}{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\,.

(xii)

The parameter
{\mathsf{d}}
, which is used in the inverse divergence operator of Proposition
A.2
to count the order of a parametrix expansion, is chosen as the smallest integer for which we have

(8.13)

\displaystyle({\mathsf{d}}-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)

\displaystyle>b(6+13\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}})+2\beta b^{2}+(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil)\left(\frac{b-1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}+\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)(9+{\mathsf{C}_{b}})\right)\,.

(xiii)

The value of
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
, which is introduced in Section
2
and used to quantify the highest order derivative estimates utilized throughout the scheme is chosen as the smallest integer such that

(8.14)

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}>(2\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}+14\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}+2{\mathsf{d}}+2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+12)2^{{n_{\rm max}}+1}\,.

(xiv)

Having chosen all the previous parameters in items (
i
)–(
xiii
), there exists a
sufficiently large
parameter
a_{*}\geq 1
, which depends on all the parameters listed above (which recursively means that
a_{*}=a_{*}(\beta,b)
), and which allows us to choose
a
an
arbitrary number
in the interval
[a_{*},\infty)
. While we do not give a formula for
a_{*}
explicitly, it is chosen so that
a_{*}^{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}
is at least twice larger than
all the implicit constants in the
\lesssim
symbols throughout the paper
; note that these constants only depend on the parameters in items (
i
)–(
xiii
) — never on
q
— which justifies the existence of
a_{*}
.

8.2.
Definitions of the
q
-dependent parameters

8.2.1.
Parameters which depend only on
q

For
q\geq 0
, we define the fundamental frequency parameter as

(8.15)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q}

\displaystyle=2^{\big\lceil(b^{q})\log_{2}a\big\rceil}\,.

Definition (
8.15
) gives that
\lambda_{q}
is an integer power of
2
, and that we have the bounds

(8.16)

\displaystyle a^{(b^{q})}\leq\lambda_{q}\leq 2a^{(b^{q})}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\frac{1}{3}\lambda_{q}^{b}\leq\lambda_{q+1}\leq 2\lambda_{q}^{b}

for all
q\geq 0
. Throughout the paper, if there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that
C^{-1}A\leq B\leq CA
, we say that
A\approx B
. In particular, the above reads
\lambda_{q}\approx a^{(b^{q})}
and
\lambda_{q+1}\approx\lambda_{q}^{b}
. It will be convenient to denote the quotient of two consecutive frequency parameters by

(8.17)

\displaystyle\Theta_{q+1}=\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q}^{-1}\approx\lambda_{q}^{b-1}\,.

The fundamental amplitude parameter is defined in terms of
\lambda_{q}
by

(8.18)

\displaystyle\delta_{q}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{1}^{(b+1)\beta}\lambda_{q}^{-2\beta}\,.

We now introduce a parameter which is defined in terms of the parameter
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}
from (
8.6
) and used repeatedly to mean “a tiny power of the frequency parameter”:

(8.19)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}

\displaystyle=\Theta_{q+1}^{\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\,.

In order to cap off our derivative losses, we need to mollify in space and time using the operators described in Section
4
. This is done in terms of the following space and time parameters:

(8.20)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{5}

(8.21)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\tau_{q}^{-1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{3}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q+1}\,.

While
\widetilde{\tau}_{q}
is used for mollification and thus for rough material derivative bounds, the fundamental temporal parameter used in the paper for sharp material derivative bounds is

(8.22)

\displaystyle\tau_{q}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{c_{0}}+6}\bigr)^{-1}\,.

Note that besides depending on the parameters introduced in (
i
)–(
xiv
), the parameters introduced above only depend on
q
, but are independent of
n
. We note that the definitions of the parameters listed so far in this subsection have not been changed from the definitions used in
[
5
]
.

8.2.2.
Parameters which depend on
q
and
n

The rest of the parameters depend on both
q
and
n
. We start by defining the frequency parameter
\lambda_{q,n}
and the intermittency parameter
r_{q+1,n}
by

(8.23)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q,n}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2^{\lceil(1+6(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma})\log_{2}\lambda_{q}\rceil},&n=0\\
2^{\lceil(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)})\log_{2}\lambda_{q}+(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)})\log_{2}\lambda_{q+1}\rceil},&1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}\end{cases}\,,

(8.24)

\displaystyle r_{q+1,n}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}2^{\lceil\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}\lambda_{q,n}+\frac{1}{2}\log_{2}\lambda_{q+1}-2\log_{2}\Gamma_{q+1}\rceil}

for
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
.
In particular, (
8.23
) shows that
\lambda_{q,n}
is a power of
2
, with

\lambda_{q,0}\approx\lambda_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{6}
and
\lambda_{q,n}\approx\lambda_{q}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{n}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}
for
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
. Similarly, (
8.24
) shows that
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,n}
is an integer power of
2
, and we have
\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\approx\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{-2}
. A consequence of these approximations are the inequalities

(8.25)

r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\leq 2\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q}}=2\Theta_{q+1}\,,\qquad r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\leq 2\Gamma_{q+1}^{-4}\frac{\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}}\,.

We recall from (
2.7c
) that the stresses
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
for
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
will be measured in terms of

(8.26)

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,n}=\begin{cases}\delta_{q+1}\Gamma_{q}^{-\mathsf{C_{R}}},&n=0\\
\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,0}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}}{\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9},&n=1\\
\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,0}\frac{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}}{\lambda_{q,n-1}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{8}\left(\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\right)^{\Upsilon(n)},&2\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}\,.\end{cases}

The function
\Upsilon(n)
is defined in (
6.6
) to quantify the number of steps required to produce
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
. As each step accumulates negligible losses, which correspond to the quantity in parentheses above, one may adhere to the heuristic that
\delta_{q+1,n}
is roughly speaking equal to
\frac{\delta_{q+1}\lambda_{q}}{\lambda_{q,n}}
. We remark that each of the parameters defined so far in this subsubsection has a new definition compared to that of
[
5
]
.

Conversely, the following three parameters remain unchanged when compared to
[
5
]
. For
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we define
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
in terms of
\mathsf{c_{0}}
by

(8.27)

\displaystyle\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{c_{0}}-4n\,.

For
n=0
, we set

(8.28)

\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{fin},0}=\frac{3}{2}\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin},

while for
1\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we define
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
inductively on
n
by using (
8.28
) and the formula

(8.29)

\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}=\left\lfloor\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{fin},\textnormal{n}-1}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-6\right)-{\mathsf{d}}\right\rfloor\,.

8.3.
Inequalities and consequences of the parameter definitions

Due to (
8.15
) we have that
\Gamma_{q+1}\geq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})^{b\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\lambda_{q}^{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\geq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})^{b\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\lambda_{0}^{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}\geq(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})a_{*}^{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}
. As was already mentioned in item (
xiv
), we have chosen
a_{*}
to be sufficiently large so that
a_{*}^{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}
is at least twice larger than all the implicit constants appearing in all
\lesssim
symbols throughout the paper. Therefore, for any
q\geq 0
, we may use a single power of
\Gamma_{q+1}
to absorb any implicit constant in the paper: an inequality of the type
A\lesssim B
may be rewritten as
A\leq\Gamma_{q+1}B
.

From the definition (
8.22
) of
\tau_{q}
and (
8.27
), which gives that
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
is decreasing with respect to
n
, we have that for all
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,

(8.30)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+6}\delta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{q}^{-1}\,.

Using the definitions (
8.18
), (
8.19
), (
8.20
), and (
8.22
), and writing out everything in terms of
\lambda_{q-1}
, we have

(8.31)

\displaystyle\tau_{q-1}^{-1}\Gamma_{q+1}^{3+\mathsf{c_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{q}^{-1}\,.

From the definition of
\widetilde{\tau}_{q}
, it is immediate that

(8.32)

\displaystyle\tau_{q}^{-1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq{\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}}\leq\tau_{q}^{-1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{3}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{q+1}}\,.

From the definitions (
8.5
) of
\mathsf{c_{0}}
and (
8.27
) of
\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}
, we have that for all
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,

(8.33)

-\mathsf{c}_{\textnormal{n}}+4\leq-1.

Next, we a list a few consequences of the fact that
\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}\gg\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
, as specified in (
8.11
). First, we note from (
8.32
) that

(8.34)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\tau}_{q-1}^{-1}\tau_{q-1}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q-1}^{3}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\lambda_{q}^{4}

where in the second inequality we have used that
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\leq\frac{3}{20b}
.

The fact that
\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}
is taken to be much larger than
\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}
, as expressed in (
8.10
), implies when combined with (
8.34
) the following bound, which is also used in Section
5
:

(8.35)

\displaystyle\left(\tau_{q}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1}\right)^{\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}}}

for all
q\geq 1
.
The parameter
\alpha
in (
8.8
) is chosen as such in order to ensure that

(8.36)

\lambda_{q+1}^{\alpha}\approx\Gamma_{q+1}.

for all
q\geq 0
.
We note that the previous seven inequalities only involve parameters which have not changed when compared to
[
5
]
.

Next, we list a number of parameter inequalities which are not the same as those in
[
5
]
. Our choice of
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}
in (
8.12
) and the assumption that
a
is chosen sufficiently large so that
\Gamma_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}>2\pi\sqrt{3}
yields

(8.37)

\displaystyle\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\leq\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q+1}}{2\pi\sqrt{3}}\right)^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}\qquad\impliedby\qquad\frac{8b}{(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}}<{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}\,.

We need a number of new inequalities to manage the Type 1 oscillation errors. The first of these is

(8.38)

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{8}\leq\begin{cases}\Gamma_{q}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\delta_{q+1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\left(\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\right)^{{\widetilde{n}})}&\mbox{if}\quad{\widetilde{n}}=0,1\\
\Gamma_{q}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\delta_{q+1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\left(\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\right)^{\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+1}&\mbox{if}\quad 2\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}\end{cases}\,.

If
{\widetilde{n}}=0
, then the inequality follows from (
8.26
), (
8.20
), (
8.23
), and (
6.6
). If
{\widetilde{n}}=1
, the inequality follows from the aforementioned inequalities and the equality

(8.39)

\frac{\lambda_{q,1}}{\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}=\frac{\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}-1}}=\frac{\lambda_{q+1}}{\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}}=\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\,,

which holds for
2\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
. Finally, if
2\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we use the aforementioned inequalities in conjunction with (
8.39
). Next, we claim that for all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,

(8.40)

\Gamma_{q}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\delta_{q+1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\left(\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\right)^{\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+1}\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\,.

The above inequality is a consequence of (
6.8
) and

\displaystyle 2\beta b(b-1)+(b-1)\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left({-\mathsf{C_{R}}}\left(\frac{1}{b}-1\right)+15+9(3+\lceil\log_{2}({n_{\rm max}})\rceil)\right)+\left(3+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil\right)\frac{b-1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}

(8.41)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad<\frac{b-1}{2}+\frac{{n_{\rm max}}}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}(b-1)\,,

which in turn follows from (
8.2b
) and (
8.6d
).
Finally, we claim that for
n
such that
n>r({\widetilde{n}})
, as defined in (
6.5
), and
n>2
,

(8.42)

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\lambda_{q,n-1}^{-1}\leq\delta_{q+1,n}\,.

If
{\widetilde{n}}=0,1
, the inequality follows from the definitions of
\lambda_{q,0}
and
\lambda_{q,1}
in (
8.23
), the definition of the
\delta_{q+1,n}
’s in (
8.26
), and (
6.6
), which guarantees that
\Upsilon(n)\geq 1
for
n\geq 2
. In the case
2\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, the inequality follows from the aforementioned inequalities combined with (
8.39
) and the fact that for
n>r({\widetilde{n}})
, (
6.7
) gives that
\Upsilon(n)\geq\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+1
.

The amplitudes of the higher order corrections
w_{q+1,n,p}
must meet the inductive assumptions stated in (
2.9a
). Towards this end, we claim that for all
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}\leq{n_{\rm max}}
,

(8.43)

\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{5}\leq\delta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,.

Indeed, the case
{\widetilde{n}}=0
follows from the definition of
\mathsf{C_{R}}
in (
8.4
), while the case
{\widetilde{n}}\geq 1
is a consequence of the definition (
8.26
) and the inequality

(8.44)

\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)\left(5+(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil)(9+{\mathsf{C}_{b}})\right)+(b-1)\frac{2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil}{{n_{\rm max}}}<\frac{b-1}{2}\,,

which in turn is a consequence of (
8.2b
) and (
8.6c
).

We will also need that

(8.45)

\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+13}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\lambda_{q,{n_{\rm max}}}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-2}\,.

The above inequality is a consequence of (
2.7a
), (
8.39
), and

(8.46)

\varepsilon_{\Gamma}\left(\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{b}+14\left(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil\right)+20-{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}<0\,,

which holds due to the choice of
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
in (
8.7
) and (
8.6a
). The inequality (
8.45
) then immediately implies that

(8.47)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+13}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-2}\,.

We claim now that
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
satisfies

(8.48)

\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{7\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+\nicefrac{{7}}{{2}}}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-2}\Theta_{q+1}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\,.

We may verify this by using (
8.25
), the definition of
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
in (
8.7
), and the inequalities

(8.49)

\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2b}+7(2+\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil)+4\leq{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-2\,,\quad\impliedby\quad 1-\frac{1}{2b}>\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(b-1)({n_{\rm max}}+1)(7\lceil\log_{2}{n_{\rm max}}\rceil+20)\,,

the second of which follows from (
8.6a
).

Next, we claim that due to our choice of
{\mathsf{d}}
, we have

(8.50)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\lambda_{q+1}\left(\Theta_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2({n_{\rm max}}+1)}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9+{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}\right)^{\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})}\lambda_{q+1}\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{{\mathsf{d}}-1}\left(\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\right)^{3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}}\leq\frac{\delta_{q+2}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{10}}\,,

and

(8.51)

(\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+5}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1})\left(\frac{\Gamma_{q+1}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}{\lambda_{q+1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}}\right)^{({\mathsf{d}}-1)}\left(\lambda_{q+1}^{4}\right)^{3\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}}\leq\frac{\delta_{q+2}}{\lambda_{q+1}^{10}}\,.

The bound (
8.50
) follows from (
8.13
) and (
8.34
), while (
8.51
) follows from (
8.50
) and the parameter inequality
\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+5}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\lambda_{q+1}^{2}
.

For estimating the stresses emerging from the divergence correctors, we shall need the bound

(8.52)

\displaystyle r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{13}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\,,

which follows from (
8.25
), (
8.38
), and (
8.40
) and implies that

(8.53)

\displaystyle\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{9}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}=r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{2}\Gamma_{q+1}^{13}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\,.

We furthermore need that

(8.54)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({\widetilde{n}})+7}\leq\Gamma_{q}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{14\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}})+7}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\,,

which in turn follows from
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}\geq\frac{b}{b-1}(8+14\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}}))
, which is a consequence of (
8.6a
) and (
6.8
).

In order to estimate the transport and Nash errors in
L^{1}
in Sections
7.7
and
7.8
, we claim that

(8.55)

\Gamma_{q+1}^{{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}}+4}\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tau_{q}^{-1}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2}\,.

In order to verify (
8.55
), we note that by (
8.18
), (
8.19
), (
8.22
), (
8.24
), (
8.26
), the definition of
{\mathsf{C}_{b}}
in (
8.3
), and the previously established parameter inequalities (
8.38
) and (
8.40
), the left side of (
8.55
) is bounded from above by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+\mathsf{c}_{0}+13}(\delta_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}\lambda_{q,{\widetilde{n}}})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\delta_{q}\lambda_{q})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+\mathsf{c}_{0}+13}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{-{\mathsf{C}_{b}}-9{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}}\lambda_{q+1}\delta_{q+2})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}(\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}\lambda_{q+1}^{2\beta b-1}\lambda_{q}^{1-2\beta})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q+1}^{-\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+\mathsf{c}_{0}+10+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{C_{R}}}\lambda_{q}^{(\beta b+\beta-1)(b-1)}(\Gamma_{q+1}^{{-\mathsf{C_{R}}}-1}\delta_{q+2})\,.

Thus, (
8.55
) holds since
\varepsilon_{\Gamma}(\frac{1}{2}{\mathsf{C}_{b}}+\mathsf{c}_{0}+10+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{C_{R}})+2\beta b<1
, in view of (
8.1
) and (
8.6e
). To estimate the transport and Nash errors in
L^{\infty}
, we finally need that

(8.56)

\Gamma_{q}^{\frac{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}}{2}}\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}+7\Upsilon({n_{\rm max}})+21+4{\widetilde{n}}}\Theta_{q}^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\lambda_{q}r_{q+1,{\widetilde{n}}}^{-1}\lambda_{q+1}^{-1}\leq\Gamma_{q+1}^{{\mathsf{C_{u}}}-1}\,,

which follows from the definition of
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
in (
8.7
), (
8.25
), and (
8.6b
).

In Remark
2.7
, have have used that

(8.57)

\lim_{(\beta,b)\to(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-,1+)}\frac{2\beta b}{(b-1)({\mathsf{C_{u}}}\varepsilon_{\Gamma}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}+2\rightarrow\infty

which is a consequence of the choice of
{\mathsf{C_{u}}}
in (
8.7
).

We conclude this section by verifying a few inequalities concerning the parameter
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
, which counts the number of available space-plus-material derivative for the residual stress
\mathring{R}_{q,n}
. This verification is the same as in
[
5
, Section 9.3]
. For all
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
we require that

(8.58a)

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,t}},2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-5\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}\,,

(8.58b)

\displaystyle 14\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{fin},\textnormal{n}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}-9\,,

(8.58c)

\displaystyle 6\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-6\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}\,,

(8.58d)

\displaystyle 6\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{ind,v}}

\displaystyle\leq\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-7\right)\rfloor\,.

for all
0\leq n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
. Additionally for
0\leq{\widetilde{n}}<n\leq{n_{\rm max}}
, we require that

(8.59)

\displaystyle\lfloor\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\left(\mathsf{N}_{\rm{fin,}{\widetilde{n}}}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}-\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}-6\right)\rfloor-{\mathsf{d}}\geq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}

holds. The inequality (
8.59
) is a direct consequence of the recursive formula (
8.29
) and of the fact that the sequence
\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}
is monotone decreasing with respect to
n
. Using (
8.28
) and (
8.29
) one may show that

\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin,n}\geq 2^{-n}\mathsf{N}_{\textnormal{fin},0}-(2{\mathsf{d}}+\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,t}+\mathsf{N}_{\rm cut,x}+8)\,.

Noting that the bounds (
8.58
) are most restrictive for
n={n_{\rm max}}
, they now readily follow from (
8.14
).

Appendix A
Auxiliary lemmas

A.1.
L^{p}
decorrelation

In order to estimate the perturbation in
L^{p}
spaces as well as terms appearing in the Reynolds stress we will need a combination of
[
5
, Lemma A.7]
and
[
5
, Remark A.9]
, which we recall next.

Lemma A.1

(
L^{p}
decorrelation with flows).

Let
p\in\{1,2\}
, and fix integers
N_{\circ}\geq{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}\geq 1
.
Suppose
f\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
and let
\Phi\colon\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a vector field advected by an incompressible velocity field
v
, i.e.
D_{t}\Phi=(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla)\Phi=0
. Denote by
\Phi^{-1}
the inverse of the flow
\Phi
, which is the identity at a time slice which intersects the support of
f
. Assume that for some
\lambda,\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\geq 1
and
\mathcal{C}_{f}>0
the functions
f
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}f\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{f}\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,N_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)

for all
N+M\leq N_{\circ}
, and that
\Phi
, and
\Phi^{-1}
are bounded as

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}f)}+\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}f)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{N}

for all
N\leq N_{\circ}
.
Lastly, suppose that
\varphi
is
(\mathbb{T}/\mu)^{3}
-periodic, and that there exist parameters
\widetilde{\zeta}\geq\zeta\geq\mu
and
\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}>0
such that

(A.1)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\varphi\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}\right)\,

for all
0\leq N\leq N_{\circ}
. If the parameters

\lambda\leq\mu\leq\zeta\leq\widetilde{\zeta}

satisfy

(A.2)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\zeta}^{4}\bigl(2\pi\sqrt{3}\lambda\mu^{-1}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}\leq 1\,,

and we have

2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4\leq N_{\circ}\,,

then the bound

(A.3)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\left(f\;\varphi\circ\Phi\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{f}\mathcal{C}_{\varphi}\mathcal{M}\left(N,N_{x},\zeta,\widetilde{\zeta}\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)

holds for
N+M\leq N_{\circ}
and
M\leq N_{\circ}-2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}-4
.

A.2.
Inversion of the divergence

Given a vector field
G^{i}
, a zero mean periodic function
\varrho
and an incompressible flow
\Phi
, our goal in this section is to write
G^{i}(x)\varrho(\Phi(x))
as the divergence of a symmetric tensor. For this purpose, we use
[
5
, Proposition A.18]
.

Proposition A.2

(
Intermittency-friendly inverse divergence).

Fix an incompressible vector field
v
and denote its material derivative by
D_{t}=\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla
. Fix integers
N_{*}\geq M_{*}\geq 1
. Also fix
{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}},{\mathsf{d}}\geq 1
such that
N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}\geq 2{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}+4
, and let
p\in\{1,\infty\}
.

Let
G
be a vector field and assume there exists a constant
\mathcal{C}_{G}>0
and parameters
\lambda,\nu\geq 1
such that

(A.4)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}G\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G}\lambda^{N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)

for all
N\leq N_{*}
and
M\leq M_{*}
.

Let
\Phi
be a volume preserving transformation of
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, such that

D_{t}\Phi=0\,\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\left\|\nabla\Phi-\mathrm{Id}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\,.

Denote by
\Phi^{-1}
the inverse of the flow
\Phi
, which is the identity at a time slice which intersects the support of
G
.
Assume that the velocity field
v
and the flow functions
\Phi
and
\Phi^{-1}
satisfy the following bounds

(A.5)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}+\left\|D^{N+1}\Phi^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{\prime N}

(A.6)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}Dv\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathrm{supp\,}G)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu\lambda^{\prime N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)\,,

for all
N\leq N_{*}
,
M\leq M_{*}
, and some
\lambda^{\prime}>0
.

Lastly, let
\varrho,\vartheta\colon\mathbb{T}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
be two zero mean functions with the following properties:

(i)

there exists
{\mathsf{d}}\geq 1
and a parameter
\zeta\geq 1
such that
\varrho(x)=\zeta^{-2{\mathsf{d}}}\Delta^{\mathsf{d}}\vartheta(x)

(ii)

there exists a parameter
\mu\geq 1
such that
\varrho
and
\vartheta
are
(\nicefrac{{\mathbb{T}}}{{\mu}})^{3}
-periodic

(iii)

there exists parameters
\Lambda\geq\zeta
,
\mathcal{C}_{*}\geq 1
, and
\alpha\in(0,1]
, such that

(A.7)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\vartheta\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{*}\Lambda^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}\left(N,2{\mathsf{d}},\zeta,\Lambda\right)

for all
0\leq N\leq\mathsf{N}_{\rm fin}
.

If the above parameters satisfy

(A.8)

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda\ll\mu\leq\zeta\leq\Lambda\,,

where by
\ll
in (
A.8
) we mean that

(A.9)

\displaystyle\Lambda^{4}\bigl(2\pi\sqrt{3}\lambda\mu^{-1}\bigr)^{{\mathsf{N}_{\rm dec}}}\leq 1\,,

then, we have that

(A.10)

\displaystyle G\;\varrho\circ\Phi=\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{H}\left(G\varrho\circ\Phi\right)\right)+\nabla P+E.

where the traceless symmetric stress
\mathcal{H}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)
and the scalar pressure
P
are supported in
\mathrm{supp\,}G
, and for any fixed
\alpha\in(0,1)
they satisfy

(A.11)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\mathcal{H}\left(G\varrho\circ\Phi\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}P\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{*}\zeta^{-1}\Lambda^{\alpha}\mathcal{M}\left(N,1,\zeta,\Lambda\right)\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)

for all
N\leq N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}
and
M\leq M_{*}
. The implicit constants depend on
N,M,\alpha
but not
G
,
\varrho
, or
\Phi
. Lastly, for
N\leq N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}
and
M\leq M_{*}
the error term
E
in (
A.10
) satisfies

(A.12)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}E\right\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{*}\lambda^{\mathsf{d}}\zeta^{-{\mathsf{d}}}\Lambda^{\alpha+N}\mathcal{M}\left(M,M_{t},\nu,\widetilde{\nu}\right)\,.

We emphasize that the range of
M
in (
A.11
) and (
A.12
) is exactly the same as the one in (
A.4
), while the range of permissible values for
N
shrank from
N_{*}
to
N_{*}-{\mathsf{d}}
.

Lastly, let
N_{\circ},M_{\circ}
be integers such that
1\leq M_{\circ}\leq N_{\circ}\leq M_{*}/2
.
Assume that in addition to the bound (
A.6
) we have the following global lossy estimates

(A.13)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}\partial_{t}^{M}v\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{3})}\lesssim\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}^{N}\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-M}

for all
M\leq M_{\circ}
and
N+M\leq N_{\circ}+M_{\circ}
, where

(A.14)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{v}\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\lesssim\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1},\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\lambda^{\prime}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}_{q}\leq\Lambda\leq\lambda_{q+1}\,.

If
{\mathsf{d}}
is chosen large enough so that

(A.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{G}\mathcal{C}_{*}\Lambda\left(\lambda\zeta^{-1}\right)^{{\mathsf{d}}-1}\left(1+\tau_{q}\max\{\widetilde{\tau}_{q}^{-1},\widetilde{\nu},\mathcal{C}_{v}\Lambda\}\right)^{M_{\circ}}\leq\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{-10}\,,

then we may write

(A.16)

\displaystyle E=\mathrm{div\,}\left(\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)\right)+\fint_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}G\varrho\circ\Phi dx\,,

where
\mathcal{R}^{*}(G\varrho\circ\Phi)
is a traceless symmetric stress which satisfies

(A.17)

\displaystyle\left\|D^{N}D_{t}^{M}\mathcal{R}^{*}\left(G\varrho\circ\Phi\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq\delta_{q+2}\lambda_{q+1}^{N-10}\tau_{q}^{-M}

for
N\leq N_{\circ}
and
M\leq M_{\circ}
.

The estimates claimed in Proposition
A.2
for
p=1
are taken as is from
[
5
, Proposition A.18]
. The definition/construction of the operators
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{R}^{*}
is independent of
p
. Then the estimates claimed in Proposition
A.2
in the case
p=\infty
follow from the proof of
[
5
, Proposition A.18]
after replacing each instance of an
L^{p}
bound for
p\neq\infty
in the proof with an
L^{\infty}
bound.
